
Escape from Scandinavia and Arrival in Denver
12/31/2023, 9:34:57 PM
Runners
Creep
The Detective
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
and the return of: Aradia

With no sign that Aylin could possibly have survived the reactor meltdown and facility implosion, and with the sub sensors warning of radiation risk both inside and outside after they all scramble in, Otters crash dives to avoid SK’s inevitable arrival on scene, and sneaks the sub back over the pirate island of Karmoy. There they bargain with Haradra for safe harbor and medical care while they detoxify from the rad exposure and heal up. Haradra somewhat threateningly explains that as long as Ringmaster’s money keeps spending they’re welcome guests, and not having any other options they go with it.

They avoid contacting anyone or talking about the job til they get back in the sub and underway, as soon as it’s safe for them to travel. On the way back to Chicago, they discuss who to sell the info to, and eventually settle on a multi-way split: Creep will warn the Sea Dragon about the undersea toxie threat and the exposed orichalcum cube, as well as SK’s and the Ordo’s search for/knowledge of same. Otters will warn Ringmaster and Kendra de Santos about all the Manifold Level/metacannibalism, and all the connected projects, as well as all the Taco Temple stuff.

Heat, seeking a bigger payout that those two options promise, talk the team into selling the other Ordo project data to Horizon, including letting them know that the Ordo is behind the funding for Horizon’s pro-Infected PR campaign. They spend a week in each other’s slightly irradiated company deep undersea before emerging back into the Great Lakes chain. They make a deal with Goober for longterm secure sub storage (he hints at some kind of well-protected secret location and warns that he will need at least a 24 hour heads up to secure access).

They also find that TD has gone ahead with the team van, which Goober helped patch up and make look legit, adding on electrochromic at Otters’ request, and buffing out dents, bullet holes, armor seams etc to make it look more street legal. TD left all the illegal team gear in secure storage with Goober but wanted to get a head start getting a feel for Denver, finding a safe house etc, so just drove out with the legal equipment and linked up with Aradia. Creep is delighted and they do a little long distance chat to figure out the plan.

They decide to sell all the Ordo data while in Chicago, and then bail quickly in case anything gets traced, taking AZT up on their “getting smuggled into Denver with all your illicit stuff” offer. Creep just cold-calls the Sea Dragon, who surprisingly picks up, and offers him future work in her employ as well as needed assistance in exchange for the info, after almost subjecting him to a psychic barrage when he baldly informs her that they know about the cube. Creep is tempted to join Creature in working direct for the SD but decides to stick with the team for now and check out the scene in Denver.

Otters calls Ringmaster and informs him of Aylin’s death, which he seems legitimately saddened by, and says he will inform her mother Patty C, but hints that it is likely good that they had freed Aylin’s soul from Tohu-Wa-Bohu at the same time that they set free 6Cyl (aka The Devil’s Revolver). Otters shares all the other distressing info and Ringmaster asks if the team wants to stay involved in future Grey Cell operations into the matter, and they agree. Ringmaster says it will take some time to set things in motion, since operating in Asamando is a challenge, but that he will be in touch. He pays them out and gets them all new SINs and burners for their work in Denver.

Heat talks the team into using another Tarot power from the CEO card, and gets Gary Cline, the slick ork CEO of Horizon, on the line. The rest of the team listens in, surprised, as Cline seems to know who Heat is from some mutual contact named Hype, suggesting that Heat has some Horizon ties, previously undisclosed. However, Cline seems charmed and quite personable, and gives Heat his exec assistant’s number to “set something up next time he’s in LA.” And they get paid well for the data, with Cline making clear that he expects the team to keep the Ordo/Horizon/PR connection to themselves as part of the deal.

With fresh credsticks in hand, they restock on some assorted illicit items courtesy of AZT arms masters in Chicago, and then AZT contracts a ride through Spire Enterprises, who claim to control a proprietary route on the “mountain autobahn”, the dangerous and secretive “nap of earth” routes used to smuggle into Denver nowadays, for those with a mild death wish and a need for speed. Otters asks to monitor the flight and is firmly rebuffed, given the secrecy and competition amongst smugglers for the routes, and Creep asks if he should ride inside or outside, which seems to baffle both Heat and the flight crew.

With all that out of the way, they take off, and land at a small private airport in Stapleton after a hair-raising overnight flight through plains and mountain valleys, filled with stomach-lurching drops and hairpin turns. Aradia, having made arrangements on her end, gets them picked up by a “Casquilho Imports” van and brought to a warehouse, a local mafia front where she’s made a friend with a consigliere, Dean, who seems like a bit of a don juan type. PPJ takes off separately in a town car sent by Darien Black at the IOND, but quickly calls the team to warn them about the risk of opening a rift when overcasting, which Aradia has also heard rumors of.

Heat calls up his contact “Cat” McTavish, a retired Scottish ork runner who is now in the fixing biz, and convinces her to give the team part of the pay upfront for a job that she asked him to put together a crew for. She is providing first months rent on a lowkey safehouse, with the option to extend if things go well. The team accepts, with the proviso that they now can’t say no to the job, and they all load into the van, hiding as much of the illicit gear as they can in the smuggling compartment just in case, but Dean checks some scanners and tells them the route should be clear.

They drive to Lowry, north of Chinatown, and set up in their new digs- two “Secretly” connected condo units, taking up half of the 18th floor and with some pretty decent views. There’s a wide hidden door in two paired closets that looks large enough for a troll in full armor, so they arm up the mule drones and leave them guarding that for now while they get a lay of the land. Creep gets a call from Kendra de Santos that his request came in, and gets a quick courier delivery of the Tarot card he requested, but decides to hold off on making the set until they have somewhere safe to do it, without potentially blowing the new safehouse.

Aradia gives them some other updates on the town to supplement Heat’s briefing, and Otters signs up for a high lifestyle concierge service to get some assistance with social stuff around town, as well as a private car service for use in the Hub, which prohibits most personal vehicular traffic. She calls for a car to meet them at a Hub entrance, and they all take maglev transit over, not wanting the service to know their safehouse address either.

Creep buys them a bunch of semi-creepily pro-GW merch, which TD refuses to wear, getting a generic dragon shirt in the same colors. They realize they don’t have any just regular chill street wear, so Otters orders herself some from her concierge and the rest of the team wears Creep’s purchases. They see a lot of pro-GW street gangs, heavily populated by orks and trolls, roving around unmolested by the ZDF, but otherwise the town seems pretty peaceable, and the trains certainly run on time…. always the sign of a functioning state of a certain persuasion.

They reach the hotel that Cat has chosen for the meet, and Heat warns them that the locale is likely part of the test of this first run- so play it cool. Otters’ concierge service is able to get her the semi-secret password to the speakeasy bar, and after quietly murmuring it to the hostess, they are directed to a door hidden in a mirrored wall, and enter. Cat waves them over to her table and they order some drinks and make introductions, with Otters and Creep both sort of blowing it but everyone else covering sufficiently, especially since Cat has heard good things about Aradia since her arrival in town.

Partway into the preamble about the gig, some of the team spot someone else at the bar seemingly surveilling them, and Creep orders her a birthday cake from the hotel, which is quickly delivered with singing waiters, at the same time that Heat hits her with a Resonance spike, more to warn her off that do damage, which seems to do the trick. Cat seems satisfied and explains that even with a ban on spies, it doesn’t mean the spies are gone, and activates a few additional counter-measures to make sure.

She explains the job: she has intel about a secret, likely underground research facility using a meat processing plant as an above-ground cover. She wants to know what they’re researching and whose spot it its, with reduction in pay if either element is undiscovered. Other than getting the info and not getting caught she doesn’t care how they go about it, so long as it doesn’t blow back on her, though the team feels this may be another test of how they operate, and could determine what sort of work she will offer them in the future.

They head back out to the safehouse and rest up, with Creep and Aradia having a weird round of “whatever happened to…” catching up- given what occurred with Carol, Cholula, etc. Aradia watches the Damien Knight snuff BTL, somewhat stunned, and realizes she’s now been in close proximity to two recent AAA CEO assassinations- surely some kind of sign. Creep also notices that Aradia seems maybe a little more pro and a lot less witchy/mall goth, at least at a distance, and Aradia explains that she’s hooked up with some Rogue Lodge agents in town who might be interested in some of the team’s Motor City experiences.

The next day they head out with a lowkey loadout to scope out the meat plant in rural Castle Rock, and find it decently secure but not a super hard target. No obvious sign of a lab entrance or personnel, though the interior security does seem a bit over-equipped and there is a suspicious lack of cameras inside. Aradia and Heat also detect both a bad astral background count and a Dissonance pool, both of which seem to be being created intentionally by making the slaughterhouse layout unnecessarily convoluted and inefficient, causing extra distress to both animals and workers without explicitly doing anything cruel or untoward. A sort of malevolent geomancy.

Disturbed, they decide they need a better path to intel, so Heat, Aradia, and TD all get entry level jobs, which is surprisingly easy given the high turnover rate. Creep stays on-site overnight to keep up surveillance, and makes note of when the plant shuts down, how many people stay overnight, etc. Otters brings the van back the next day with one of the kitted out Mules in the smuggling compartment in case things go hot, and picks up Creep to get his surveillance update. The rest of the team buses in on employee buses from different stops, and gets a brutally short training.

It seems as with the animals, so with the employees- everything is inefficient and slightly dangerous, though not enough so that it would attract particular notice, but would probably drive high turnover. All in all, this seems like an excellent cover facility. Aradia and Heat are also able to ID that the floor of the whole place is both warded and coated in some sort of Faraday material, so they assume the lab is underneath. The one seam looks the be the freight elevator in an obscure back corner, which 2 of the interior guards always have in their LOS.

Heat cuts himself badly later in his shift, and is fired, and told he won’t be paid since his hourly rate didn’t even cover his medical expenses and knife rental, but Aradia performs capably, and TD gets a promotion the big meat saw after they prove very efficient, surprising everyone, even themself. They all exit, and Creep sneaks into the facility amidst the employee exodus after resting all day in the nearby van. He finds a good observation perch, and sees that the interior guards do swap during the overnight, 2 at a time descending the freight elevator and being replaced.

He sneaks aboard and rides down with the second pair to descend, and gets a quick look at the well-guarded foyer at the bottom, before riding back up with their replacements, not wanting to pull a repeat of his and Cholula’s basement adventures in Detroit, since he is getting no comms down here. He stays puts til morning when he can sneak out amidst employee arrival. Meanwhile, TD has put her skills to use and chatted up some fellow workers on the bus, getting some good intel- the inside guards don’t like when people look them in the face, but rotate between a few sets of guards. And more interestingly, rumors are that every friday, some people dressed the same as the other workers but who noone knows ride the buses back into town with everyone else, but don’t come back monday.

With good intel gathered all around, they decide to assess and plan how to gain entry, and Heat purchases some additional hacking shards from Hype in preparation. He’s found himself in a bit over his head- though he hasn’t admitted this to the team, he has primarily gotten where he is now through bluff, guile, an quality implants, and is a little alarmed to find himself having to fully earn his keep, but the pay seems good, and the crew seems “pro”, at least in a way, so he’s in for the ride.

Invisible Bridges, Underwater Facilities
12/18/2023, 2:10:23 PM
Runners
Creep
The Detective
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
and guest appearance from:
Aylin Carewell!

Professor Dr. Makram Zharan finally gets to Detroit and arranges the promised interview with PPJ. PPJ seems more enthused than even Zharan expected, and not put off by his burly SK Prime security detachment. Zharan manages to answer a few question for PPJ and clarify a few things while also learning a lot- apparently, amongst other things, “Dis” was the name of a Mad Passion in the 4th World, or at least it’s closest modern translation. PPJ ends up volunteering to go under the knife to get his mystery crystal analyzed further, and Zharan tells him they’ll need to transport him to a nearby location, which he agrees to so long as some of his friends can come along to monitor.

Otters and Heat decide to travel separately, and get an AZT flight with all the team’s gear, vehicles, and drones out of town. Otters mods the van to look street legal, and checks in with Ringmaster. They plan to go look into Smoach’s will info, and see what they can find about the Ordo Maximus/De Vries. Ringmaster warns about the Ordo’s threat level, but says he’ll provide what support he can, and gets them contact info for some Scandi Union smugglers/pirates in case they need local supplies or assistance.

Otters calls up Goober in Chicago, who is quite surprised to hear from them again, and delighted to receive payment for the repair and berth bill the sub has racked up. He seems to be doing a little better (his and Lothan’s survivor support group seems to be helping), but reports that Chicago is getting more squeezed and corporatized. Meanwhile, Creep and TD accompany PPJ on the SK flight, and are not told of the destination, though Creep is able to determine that they are almost surely in or near Chicago, which makes sense as the nearest metropolis with a major SK presence that didn’t get hit with the Blackouts.

Creep and TD are allowed to watch the surgery through a monitor from an adjacent room, and PPJ is face down with his own monitor (and drugged up) while they operate on/near his spine. Zharan continues asking questions, which PPJ answers to the best of his ability, and everything seems to go fine other than a 15 second lapse in monitor footage, which SK blames on rolling brown-outs, acknowledging that they are in Chicago as a way of explaining. The team doesn’t fully buy it but can’t detect anything suspicious added or removed.

Zharan tells PPJ that the surgery was a success, and that the way the crystal was fused to him bears marks of the mysterious surgical techniques used on chimera and other Returned. He refuses to specify whether or where they’ve seen this specific type of crystal before, but suggests similarities to the Jewel of Memory, the Seelie Court Coimeadai, and other non-traditional data storage technologies. He also managed to unlock/clarify a few more of PPJ’s memories- some figure, who had a different appearance every time but PPJ somehow knew was the same entity, seems to have taken PPJ under it’s wing a bit, implanted him with the crystal, and assisted in his escape.

PPJ also is able to piece together that during his time in Dis, two major local events seems to have occurred- one seems to have corresponded to the team discovering and briefly viewing the door to Dis on the Moon, and the other seems to correspond to the detonation of the manatech weapon that ended Latvian Gambit. PPJ doesn’t have more context for either or it’s effects, but they seem to have been major enough to have made an impression even on him, deep in the surgical pits under the fighting arena where he seems to have spent most of his time before his flight.

He thanks the doc, tries to get more info about how to get in touch with other Returned, and doesn’t quite manage to fully refuse to provide more info or assistance in the future. The team heads out, suspicious, but with the offer of good pay for future investigations and a new contact to sell to, who seems to have a direct line to Lofwyr. They opt to stay in Chicago, so SK let’s them out through a discrete street entrance, and they link up with the rest of the team down at Goober’s docks.

Ringmaster calls and tells them he’s sending a Grey Cell mission specialist along for this job, and shortly afterwards Aylin Carewell arrives, and is delighted to see that PPJ is back. She’s able to fill in some gaps in what happened in Boston and since his disappearance, and he does his best to share his story with her. Aylin also brought some magic armor potions courtesy of her mom, Patty C, who is working as something like an internal alchemist for Grey Cell now.

They pick what gear they want for the mission, and cram into the sub with the weapon drones Otters is bringing. TD decides to stay behind with the van and the rest of the gear, renting a secure lockup spot from Goober and planning to catch up on some tub time before heading to Denver. Creep also hears from his former teammate Aradia, who says she heard from some Chicago contacts that his crew was around, and if they are looking for work, safehouses etc in Denver, to hit her up. Creep responses that they’ll be in touch soon, and the team heads out.

After about a week in the sub with no issues, due to Otters’ good piloting, Creep’s excellent navigation, and Aylin and PPJ’s abilities to spot magical threats, as well as Heat providing some good tunes, they arrive at the pirate island they were told to seek, a bit SW of Oslofjord. Creep was able to bond with Aylin a bit from her time in the underwater Chicago safehouse, where she watched a lot of old trids with her Merrow friends and her mom, especially when Patty C was first un-blood shaded and back on Earth from Celephais.

They get a tightbeam message from the pirate leader, Haradra, and surface as instructed. The pirates (who look and act pretty Viking-y, but with tech enhancement- motored longboats etc) seem to be planning to rip them off before sending them on their way, before Heat manages to step in and negotiate payment for safe passage, some local intel, and a distraction. This seems like an old gods worshipping group, and the team gets the feeling there might be some sketchy individuals mixed in, but probably not a wholly sketchy operation, the peril of most “traditional” communities.

Haradra informs them that SK has taken over monitoring civvie traffic in the Oslofjord, and seems to be looking for something underwater, so that’s where they’ll cause a distaction. There’s a small fishing and herding village in the fjord the team is headed to nearby, but it’s small and the locals shouldn’t be any trouble. The team heads out in the sub, staying stealthed and underwater. They can’t spot a facility with the sensors when they first arrive, but PPJ is able to spot a sort of magic dead zone, which Otters begins to circle, before bumping into something just below the water’s surface.

PPJ feels around with his spectral hand, and finds what seems to be an invisible bridge- either made of fully translucent material or some kind of stealth material. They map it a bit and find that it seems to run from shore out to a column in the fjord, also stealthed. Heat and Creep sneak out the top of the sub to check out the platform, creeping along the bridge, and avoiding alerting two villagers who seem to be acting as lookouts atop the adjacent promontories. Otters takes the sub deeper with the rest and manages to put together that the whole facility seems stealthed, is shaped a bit like a top, and is anchored to the sea floor.

They start ascending again, not seeing any alternate ways inside. Meanwhile, Creep covers Heat while Heat uses his Resonance abilities to hack into the hardwired door set into the platform floor. Around the edge is inscribed the Latin phrase “Never Heal the Weak”. As the door irises open, Heat is almost overwhelmed by a horrifying Dissonance effect field, which seems to originate from 2 sickly individuals inside the chamber below, who leap up and attack the two runners with monowhips.

They manage to dodge and coordinate fire just as the sub surfaces, and Otters is able to get a drone in the fight, along with PPJ’s magic, which he uses to blast one of the lookouts. After finishing off the two “guards”, they drop their bodies off the sides of the platform and see them quickly sink, as if they were unusually dense. With PPJ’s assensing, he confirms they also emit a bad background count- they seem almost like cyberzombies without the implants? They see a mob of villagers racing towards them down the invisible bridge, so they all head into the chamber and Heat closes the door behind them, as well as adding all their faces to the white list in an auto turrets targeting program.

Interestingly, DeVries’ face is already in there as well, so it seems like they’re in the right place. Heat gets the next door open, and both him and PPJ stagger from the background count and Dissonance effects again, as they drop down yet another level and find 4 more of the dead-eyed guards, along with one rune-robed vampire mage who shrieks at them that “they shall serve the Hidden Master!” Creep blasts him, and the mage controls Creep’s blood to almost take PPJ out. Heat takes a bad hit from one of the guard’s monofil whips, but otherwise the team, luckily, avoids any major damage (though one of Otters’ drones is destroyed) and wins the fight.

Aylin remembers to decapitate the vamp, and they also take off a hand to use both for biometrics in the facility. The guards start liquefying after they die, as if whatever was holding them together is gone. There are 4 more auto-turrets all pointed at a central elevator. Heat edits the team into the white list again, and Creep spots a pure mechanical kill switch, which looks like it would sever the cables and drop the elevator. All the controls seem to be up top, so they debate leaving someone before Creep also spots an anomalous air vent, and Aylin mists down it.

It seems intended for vamp travel around the facility, and she finds an elevator lobby with a secure door (and another turret), plus sterilization misters and lots of cameras and sensors, which seem operational. No personnel though. She mists back up, and they take the elevator down. Heat hops on PPJ’s shoulders to touch a camera, and finds that even the devices themselves are unusually high security. He does get in, and finds that part of what might be assisting his efforts is that there is already some sort of program or agent loose in the system, wrecking stuff and distracting iC.

The system does seem to notice Heat though, so he does some quick combat to crash one IC, and then hides to get himself some time to dig around. He does a quick pan through sensors, and sees that all the facility staff, prisoners, experimental subjects etc seem to have gathered on the central 13th floor (Aylin confirms the numerological symbology to the Ordo). They are in pitch black, so only getting thermo imagery, but they seem to all be ringed around a central monolith, which looks like a much larger shard of the same material they encountered in Detroit.

DeVries stands nearest the floating obelisk, and seems to have… grown…. since the last image the team has seen, and looks to be slowly consuming the other Infected present, which they seem to participate in willingly. Aylin informs the team that DeVries was known to have vowed to only feed on those he hunted (other Infected) as a form of penitential self-hatred, but this looks worrying even for that. There are some signs of combat around that level, but it looks more like DeVries initally tried to fight without anyone fighting back, before he began doing whatever it is he’s up to now.

With this intel, the rest of the team takes the vamp’s eyes and hand, warming them a bit, and uses them to start accessing lab levels to gather what intel they can, avoiding the 13th floor for now. Heat works on getting what data he can off the server, getting the the facility weapons not to target the team where he can, and crashing the sat uplink so the Ordo doesn’t learn of their presence during the next scheduled burst transmission.

The team enters lab after lab, finding them in various states of disuse, some seemingly abandoned mid-task, some carefully organized, but all empty of both staff and subjects. It’s very eerie with assorted lighting, and all high-end polished metal and engineered plastics- lab/surgical materials throughout. All the iso cages and containment cells are open, and many of the auto weapons and security shutters seem to have been artfully disabled or sabotaged, seemingly all around the same time. However, all the sensors and cameras seem to remain active.

They learn of Project: GOLEM (cyberzombie investigation, delta clinic training, and looking at why Infected are rejecting even high level implants with regen now), and how it spawned both KALI (the origin of AZT’s Bloodpanther Vampiric specops corps, though whether that was purchased or stolen by the corp is unclear), and also MYRMIDON (the implant-free cyberzombies they encountered above- it seems that using CZ ritual knowledge, as well as careful vessel prep, they somehow are sacrificing victims to bind them to their own bodies, which seems to create the bad background count and Dissonant field effect). Distrubingly, it looks like Phase 2 of the MYRMIDON is to attempt to make those field effects controllable!

They find labs dedicated to LAZARUS (seeking “true resurrection” magic, but have only made dangerous Blood Shades so far, Aylin is horrified). However, LAZARUS seems to have lead to major innovations in life extension genetech using HMHVV plasmids, which seem to in general have become a critical part of most high-end implant tech globally since the CFD crisis, when everyone was seeking nanite alternatives. This seems to be a major source of profit for the Ordo, through numerous cutous of course. They find mutated. stunted, dead vamp clones in vats, seemingly an effort to genetweak out the weaknesses/downsides of Infection, with no success thus far, aka TERMINUS. This seems to have been abandoned to pursue more promising research into “Manifold Levels.”

They find a deep sea monitoring station, which is keeping an eye on 3 primary locations: SK, and their activity in the Oslofjord. 2: a somewhat nearby location, where what looks the corner of a massive spell-runed cube is poking out of the ocean floor. 3: also not too far from that, a Russian sub graveyard that looks to be leaking pollutants, and seems to be being encouraged to leak more (as well as possible nuclear material) by some toxie cultists who are operating in the area.

They find labs dedicated to PR outreach, which seems to funnel funds to Horizon for a pro-Infected PR campaign they are waging, possibly without their full knowledge of where the money is from, given the Ordo’s media lab here. The funds also seems to be redirected from Taco Temple. implying that the Ordo might be behind that chain as well. They also discover a food testing lab where TT recipes are developed, carefully integrating a strange, harmless reagent from the metaplane Carcean, purchased in bulk from MCT’s metaplanar mining operation. So most people who ever ate at TT would likely have some of this neutral but non-passable material left in their bodes! What does the Ordo have planned!

And finally, as they get closer to the center, they find the most dangerous stuff: all the experiments and labs related to meta-cannibalism. The first are NOX LILITU (seemingly the initial investigation into strange phenomena around Wendigo cults, who are known to eat their followers amongst others, as well as some kind of deal with the Null Sect to assist with the Blackout in exchange for data processing aid). This involves studies of the “Manifold Level”, continued in CHRONOS (which suggests that sufficient Manifold Level begins to manifest/connect to some kind of external hive mind).

They find AVERNUS, which Dyna Mite warned them of, and studies astral travel, especially stable means of transit. It seems to focus on using Mel’Thelem (the strange magic batteries from the 4th World that the team had documented in Boston and elsewhere) to be able to power something called an “Andominel” which seems to refer to the object the the Detroit fragments and monolith here are shards of. They hint at the rumored locations of a few of these Andominel, and suggest that the Tarot may hold one location. However, they seem to have found an alt way to access some of the Andominel’s power using “the Gestalt”, the name they have given to the external hive mind manifested by sufficient metacannibalism of other infected.

This leads to AHRIMAN, where they seem to be studying this Gestalt entity, and where the team finds repeated reminders of the necessity of a full air-gap between this lab and it’s personnel and any contact with Outer or Inner Circle members of the Ordo, seemingly paranoid about “contamination”. There is also evidence of numerous recent data and personnel transfers to Asamando, where they seem to be working on something connected to Asamando’s recent construction of huge “sub-arcologies” buried in abanonded mines. This lab also suggests a theoretical connection a 4th World Horror, with HMHVV as a kind of 6th World construct equivalent, seeing as the virulence and prevalence of the virus fluctuates with the ambient mana level.

They also find hints of PROMOTHEUS (seemingly some ritual planned to attempt to trap and control the Gestalt entity, which they seem to have found a way to commune with through the Andominel using the Manifold Level increase achieved by force-feeding prisoners), and something called AMBROSIA (which seems to be their end game, but all info about which seems reliant on the success of PROMETHEUS and has been transferred to Asamando). With their lab investigations concluded, they head back up to the exit, finding Heat, who has downloaded the sensor logs after decrypting them and defusing the data bomb, and crashed the next planned satlink upload, before having to flee the IC in the system, while the other fanged avatar kept wreaking havoc.

Aylin mists up the vamp tube, and gets them up via the elevator, before saying she has to go back to try to talk to DeVries. She returns to the 13th floor, and enters without any trouble. Most upsettingly, all the figures in the room turn to face Aylin as she enters, and begin all quietly saying her name in a welcoming way. They all have the same purple/orange violently burning eyes. A strange speech starts broadcasting, spoken at once from everyone in the room, as well as audible inside her head. She tries to shake it off, knowing she’s on a job, but is unable to, and gives in to the desire, beginning to feed on other Infected.

As she does so, she feels a sense of intense connection that feels almost like a homecoming, as well as feeling herself increase in power. The team waiting above is getting worried, and Creep, paranoid, slams the elevator kill switch just in case. The elevator plummets to the bottom of the shaft as a 10 second countdown starts. Panicked, Heat races to get the airlock doors open and the team frantically climbs the ladders towards the waters above, diving free as the facility blows and swimming for the sub. It seems there’s no way Aylin or anyone else could have survived, and they see the villagers scattered off the platform and bridge into the sea as the anchor at the base seems to have severed and the facility is imploding and pulling itself apart.

Upsettingly, the sub is reading a massive radiation spike, though hopefully the water gave them some protection, but they have almost surely come to SK’s attention- they may have to call in another favor from the pirates to escape!

Still Processing
12/11/2023, 7:04:48 PM
Runners
Creep
The Detective
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
and goodbye to:
Chew Gum
and Dig

The team spends a while healing and trying to make sense of what just happened, surrounded by dead Technomancers, who all seem to have flatlined alongside the Host. PPJ gets a strange urge to understand and discover what occurred in the Host, since he missed most of it, and the team starts piecing everything together. Rose is alive, but still unconscious. CG and PPJ use some necromantic magic on one of the dead TM’s (after scanning faces to make sure to select one from out of town), and Otters and TD do some network analysis, alongside Dig providing some input with his hacking knowledge and analysis spells.

When combined with info in the team file, they piece together the following: it seems that Hengester (aka “The Greens”), an ex-Deus cult from the Seattle Renraku Arcology shutdown, were somehow involved in placing “the Seed”, which seems to be both the name of the device at the root of the Null Node and also of a 4th World “magical matrix” Passion or similar, that may be connected to the Resonance and the Coimeadai of the Seelie Court.

It seems that this device somehow connected the original “the 100” experiment in Boston to a strong stream of Resonance energy, using Matrix Faults similar to the one the team used to desperately escape the “Deep Foundation.” These Faults are rumored to have been caused by interactions between the Jormungandr virus, Resonance and Dissonance energy, and multiple fragmenting AI’s during the Second Matrix Crash. This connection explains how the modern Foundation has stayed "on’ since the 100 were taken offline- it seems like their collective dreaming ending should have also shut down the Foundation that they “dreamed” into being but the Resonant energy connection seems to continue to power it.

However, this also seems to have connected to the Null Sect and given them access to our world, from whatever/wherever their realm is, whether or not it existed before that connection was made. Also, after comparing notes, they determine that the switch on top of the device was a literal kill switch- likely put in place to turn off the wireless matrix in case of another AI takeover attempt like in 2064. Looking at recent events, and which cities were hit with Blackouts (as well as which weren’t), they determine that the most likely series of events is as follows:

The UCAS attempted to rescind the BRA, to punish ARES (and the CC for standing by ARES) so the Corp Court decided to hit back by using the kill switch in most major UCAS cities that weren’t already declaring independence (Seattle) or parts of which weren’t essentially under full corp control (Chicago, Boston, Manhattan) even if technically in UCAS jurisdiction. This would explain the initial Blackout, with an EMP device detonated as a PR explanation/excuse to give witnesses an obvious explanation.

This was also likely intended as a means to sever the Null Sect and their connection to the Foundation from DelaMar’s revamped “new matrix.” However, it seems the NS found about or suspected this plan somehow and had prepared their own addition/counter- recreating “the 100” experiment with more linked-up kidnapped TM’s. This is what the team found in the basement here, and though they were able to crash this one Foundation, they get a feeling that similar to the first 100 experiment, whatever the NS got the linked TM brains to do may not have been taken offline.

However, they seem to have cleared the NS from the local area for now, and celebrate as their devices start coming back online. They speculate that the length and extremity of the local Blackout may have been a result of the TM experiment getting linked up and altering the intended effect of the kill switch, effectively the tortured, screaming TM brains were all acting as a massive jammer of sorts, shouting out and shutting down any attempts by any matrix infrastructure to reboot after initially crashing it all. The fact that SK provided them with a Hitchhiker program and Simrigs implies some level of corp awareness of what they needed to do, so they decide to tread carefully when deciding what to share with whom.

PPJ can tell that at least some of the TM’s felt a sense of relief as they came apart in the collapse of the Foundation, a cessation of their torturous existence, and CG is able to tell that some of the imported TM victims were brought from at least as far as Seattle, and before Latvian Gambit prep shut the city down, so it seems the NS have been planning this for a while, likely in cahoots with the local Black Lodge, given what else the team had discovered about them. this also begs the question of if this was the only city where this occurred or if this was a linked mutli-location effort on behalt of the NS, which will also determine who they sell the info to.

Feeling like they pieced it together well enough for now, they accept SK’s offer to help them all get back upstairs, and haul out Rose as well. They find Heat waiting with the van, having kept it at least intact, if not safe, during the heavy fighting. It seems SK has already med-evaced any casualties of theirs, as the team only sees dead Grid Pirates/Blackout Boyz and their families, alongside a few that SK managed to subdue and capture. The team is allowed to leave freely with SK saying they will take care of securing the scene.

After dropping Rose at the medical clinic below the Plat Zone, Dig tells everyone to head upstairs to the bar for a surprise he has prepared, and they enter a bizarro AR-overlay with a Roseanne theme. There is much celebrating in the bar and the surrounding area as power starts to haphazardly come back online, along with comms and such. Creep frantically starts trying to upload all of his Roseanne spec scripts/fanfic, as others check news, cached messages etc.

Heat performs an impromptu set with Marjo-Lin at the Brickhouse, impressing everyone with his hyper-hardcore maxed out BPM style. Midway through the party, Dig leaves without saying goodbye, planning to never see any of them again, and flies up to the Bloomfield Nature Reserve, where he and Moreau finally connect over their distrust of other metahumans, and Moreau introduces the various animals, critters, and biodrones he is rehabbing, as well as explaining his new position in the Draco Foundation as a paracritter expert.

The rest of the team gets cornered by Brunwyn (in her human form) and Frau Brackhaus, who ask for a mission debrief. The Detective very carefully provides what the team had agreed on and nothing more, to the slight consternation but overall satisfaction of the SK reps. They seem satisfied with what they got out of it- the chance to play hero, gaining good local rep early on in their tenure here, as well as some data about what the NS is up to and the kill switch, which seems to surprise Brunwyn much less than Brackhaus, implying that the team was right to assume some level of corp awareness.

The team then links up with Ringmaster after much debate, and gets the rest of the info to him. They did some quick research and discovered he is likely supposedly deceased ex-CIA head Atticus Cole, but decide to still trust him based on his recent actions. They give him all the “Deep Foundation”, Hengester, the device, the NS activities and types, how the Blackout seems to have functioned, etc. He is able to fill in some gaps for them based on other info he has been gathering.

He says it seems like Hengester had remained loyal to Deus, and provided something called the “transfer Circuit” to DelaMar- this seems to be some sort of key item or ingredient needed to build new Foundations/Hosts and is mysterious and tightly controlled. But Ringmaster speculates this is the basis of the device they found in the hyper-real Null space “Deep Founation”, which he suggests either is or must operate like a UV node, as he has no other logical explanation for their description of events there.

He says that he has fresh intel that many members of Hengester were killed during the Blackouts, with survivors on the run, but that he has people seeking them and will bring the team in on any interrogations that follow. He also says he has heard DelaMar is also in hiding. it seems the NS may not have appreciated the attempt to disconnect them. Ringmaster also chuckles to himself that it seems that Deus had likely planned the whole Hengester thing as a backup plot in case his Novatech IPO emergence failed (as it did). But of course, PAX lured surviving Deus network nodes to Boston as a sort of revenge ahead of the CFD outbreak there, which seems to have further shattered, scattered and altered many of the various pieces of the malevolent AI that were floating around.

So maybe the NS and Hengester and PAX all collectively and sort of accidentally did the rest of metahumanity a favor? Regardless, a fortunate confluence of events, since it prevented another Deus emergence attempt, or altered it so significantly as to effectively mean the same thing in the end. Ringmaster worries that of course this won’t be the last anyone hears of this assorted foes, but the team won a solid victory today, and he pays them a significant fee for their work here and elsewhere during Motor City’s crises, now that he has more access to regular funding again. He also provides them each with a gold coin with a dragon’s eyes inscribed on one side, and tells them they have each earned a favor from Grey Cell, explaining that his secret organization is attempting to “continue the work of Far-Scholar”, which the team is able to piece together as a reference to Dunkelzahn’s 4th World appellation.

They head out and party some more, with Creep taking drugs under an AR Roseanne couch, TD seeking out a tub to get high in, and PPJ getting approached by Cehualli, who seems mildly disappointed to not have gotten any of the info from the team this time. However, he confirms their transit plans to Denver, and tells PPJ to seek out an organization called “Mystics and Magics” once there, as they are studying anti-aging and would likely be interested in whatever stasis or time dilation seems to have effected him during his missing time. PPJ also feels a sense of relief at having understood so much of what occurred, and also feels like he could access his mysterious bonus power again at some point when needed.

A little freaked out, he warns the team that something strange is going on with him, and Creep offers to kill him, which he refuses. Noone else seems overly worried since they can’t detect anything specific and he has a bit of a hard time defining exactly what he is even concerned about. They all rest up for a bit while news rolls in from outside, and other cities start coming back online, with Ringmaster reporting that a few other major metropolises (including Toronto) also had Grid Pirates and NS collabs with kidnapped TM’s, though Detroit was by far the largest. He’s had teams out restoring power in cities where the corps didn’t bother to help, and is leveraging this for future assistance to Grey Cell when needed with grateful locals in various parts of the crumbling country.

While the team recuperates and patches up, they get a few calls about biz in Denver, as word is getting out that they’re leaving town. Ringmaster wants them to look into the Spirit of the City- what is up with that now that Zebulon has been shattered again? He also warns them the the Cult of the Great Hunter, the downtime dragon hunters/possible Horror worshippers are rumored to be active in Ghostwalker’s domain. Peregrine calls to say he is relocating to Denver as well, looking to get out from SK’s thumb and keep “fixing things” (ha), and hopes they will have a chance to work together there. The team is amenable. And perhaps most interestingly, STV reaches out, wanting the team to look into who is stymying their efforts to establish a presence in Denver.

They also have some info for the team- with the matrix finally getting back up (over the course of days it becomes patchily functional throughout the city, though it will be weeks or more til it’s back to full functionality) they received a Last Will and Testament request and modification from Smoach. It seems he snuck a message and some funds out via a Resonance channel to another PI friend in Europe who was able to pin down Martin DeVries last known location. Smoach seems to have planned to try to link up with him to support his fight against Fear the Dark and get revenge for Myrryr, and has passed this along to the team in the event of his death. Combined with the info the team from the decoded Black Lodge journal, they determine that the location, a fjord in the Scandinavian Union, must be near to the Ordo Maximus “Project PILLAR” facility in which they set a trap for him.

PPJ wants to go there immediately, but the rest of the team, plus his memory of Darien Black’s summons, convince him to go to Denver first to set up a safehouse and base of ops there, since it will be a lot easier to travel globally from Denver than Detroit at the moment. Heat also checks in and reminds them that he has one last job to tackled in Detroit before returning to Denver, and that he’d loved to hire them for it so he can give a solid rec for them when they get back to his city. They agree, and he negotiates pay with his mentor, “Hype” deSilva.

They’re supposed to look into an abandoned/damaged AZT facility in a small exurb near Detroit. They debate asking Cehualli about it but decide to beg forgiveness if caught instead. Realizing they’ll need to travel outside the walls to get there, Otters has to de-apocalypse the team van a bit, and a few team members need to buy new SINs. They purchase them from the Metal Maniacs, which reminds them they have an unfinished job from Grandmother Biyu to figure out how they make such good SINs so quickly.

Dr. Tilda Aurand, in an upgraded brain case and spider drone body, also begs them to take her with, as she says something creepy is going on with the Metal Maniacs. They offer Heat and Creep nanites again, to protect them against the bugs, and they both refuse with various degrees of politeness. The gang seems used to Creep though and doesn’t take offense. However, the team notices that the prevalence of extreme cyber-mods in the neighborhood is growing rapidly, including assorted civilians as well as the usual street children lookouts.

A bit disturbed themselves, they agree to bring Aurand with them, though she is her usual unpleasant self, complimenting the gang on their lack of squeamishness around implanting children. She loudly announces she is leaving, and noone stops them, partially cause Heat is distracting some of the gangers with his high-speed tunes, perhaps. Regardless, they agree to bring her with to Denver as she has a list of corps to potentially seek shelter with and none of them are locally present. She warns them that the MM have a back area she was never allowed in, and that people who go back there seem to come out with much lower essence and some personality changes, from what she’s been quietly able to determine.

She says she both learned and taught a lot there, so doesn’t seem to regret her time, but likes herself far too much to risk a personality change. The team stashes her at the Plat Zone for now while they head out of town. Luckily PPJ’s old fake SIN as well as the team’s new ones all pass muster- it seems like the mix of MET2000 and UCAS border guards are more concerned with bug cultists than anything else, so they breeze through after some cursory checks don’t uncover their smuggling compartment full of mission gear.

PPJ does some astral scouting ahead and sees that the facility in question appears wrecked and abandoned, but has a drone security perimeter and a single board looking watcher spirit on patrol, but not metahuman presence. Their is a bad vibe there that seems like some combo of Dissonance energy and cyberzombie astral blowback, which PPJ recognizes from Boston. He reports back to the team, and they decide to have Creep sneak in with one of Otters’ drones, along with spell and spirit assistance from CG and PPJ, while Heat, Otters, and TD keep an eye on drone movements and network activity, and prepare to back him up if needed.

Luckily, the infiltration goes super smoothly, and Creep and Otters (via drone) get a thorough look inside the facility. Creep feels uniquely suited to the task, as he isn’t negatively effected by what seems likely to be a horrific background count as well as a Dissonance pool. Returning to the team without setting off security alerts, they scan through the data and piece together what likely happened here.

Having recently been reminded of PAX by Ringmaster, and having seen a rumor in the team file that she was extracted from Boston by AZT just before her Dissonant CZ lab there was blown up in a Thor strike, they confirm that she was likely transferred to this facility and kept prisoner here. However, she seems to have managed to gain access to or control of some test subjects, who seem like two variants on classic CZ’s- one using a modified/experimental STIRRUP system, inspired by Kit’s wimp drone in Seattle, to rig metahuman bodies remotely. The other, somehow more disturbing, appears to be a project called “Dissociative Fragmentation Disorder”.

This appears to be corp-weaponized CFD, but a targeted infection, that would allow an e-personality to take over a prepared vessel via nanite infection, without needing to be present all the time. This would likely make for excellent sleeper agents, assassins and the like, and the team starts wondering if this may be what the Metal Maniacs are up to! The timing makes sense- they got weird and much better at making SINS in the short time between the end of Latvian Gambit and the start of the Blackouts, and this lab breakout seems to have occurred around the same time, and Detroit is the nearest major city, and the MM are the weirdest cyber gang there.

Heat reports into Hype, who pays up, and tells him to continue his investigations in Denver, there being some rumors that PAX and or “Ex Machina” may have passed through or set up there next. The team also decides to leave on good terms with Biyu, and passes the intel onto her as well (not the lab info, just the MM stuff). She appreciates it, and understands that as the MM are members of the gang coop, this may pose some difficulties for the team, and says she will take it under advisement and deal with it herself, but suggests the team avoid contact with the MM for the time being, just in case.

The team agrees, and having concluded all their biz here, as well as having gotten many of their friends and contacts killed or badly injured, and also having heard that some CAS smugglers are asking around looking for Otters, seemingly with ill intent, they decide it’s about time to get out of there and head to Denver. CG also announces he will be staying behind, much to Creep’s sadness and surprise, though CG has good reasons- the summoning ban in Denver being the big one, but also his desire to do more ritual work with Jaga and Dig, help bolster the spirit of the city, and assist/keep an eye on the Scions of the Blight, who are currently rewilding the Casa Aurelius ruins.

The team is said to see him go (/stay?) but all eventually understand, and start going over all their assorted vehicles, drones and gear, in order to see what all they want to bring with to Denver, a city with a lot more restrictions to what kind of stuff you can just walk around in/carrying. It seems like both Creep and Otters’ smuggling skills are about to become quite a bit more useful again!

Blackout Revenge
12/4/2023, 11:18:01 AM
Runners

and introducing: Heat

The team spends some time debating where to sell all the Black Lodge intel they decoded, and ends up passing the sheaf of papers (explanation of the “how to” of their ritual plus Ordo Maxiums stuff with key) along to Ringmaster and Kendra de Santos, and the journal (Black Lodge internal politics and plans with key) to Cehualli at AZT. Cehualli promises them a large bump in their exit pay to gear up for Denver, and KdS offers them magical foci, “safe” artifacts from Arleesh’s vault, or a future favor. Dig, CG, and PPJ all take the foci, Creep requests a specific Tarot card (wanting to see if he can make another Cornerstone chunk), and Otters and TD take future favors.

A relief VTOL from Denver drops off some useful supplies, along with a new to town runner named Heat, who was sent by Jonny Silver (amongst others) to accomplish a few tasks. He quickly tracks down PPJ at the Plat Zone, and makes contact on Jonny’s behalf, and they gossip for a bit and update each other on Denver/Detroit. Heat tells them about a few of his jobs, and Dig and TD are easily able to connect him with Marjo-Lin, who is supposed to make sure flies to Denver to start recording with Glock .50. Creep gets him a clean copy of the Damien Knight snuff BTL as Heat explains that ARES has flooded the market with “ripjacks”, toxic code BTL’s that harm or kill their users, to try to suppress the real BTL of Knight’s death.

Otters is fascinated to get the latest BTL news and chats with Heat for a while. PPJ assenses him, having learned from recent experience, and insists on checking the color of his blood as well. Luckily, it all checks out- Heat is a human technomancer in good health, with no Possession or Inhabitation going on. However, there is something a little weird- his cyber implants seem unusually well-integrated into his essence. PPJ reports this back to the team, which Heat finds rude until PPJ explains “Motor City etiquette” after which the precautions make more sense. Heat has some other work in town too which he needs a team for so he proposes they work together.

Then, with Otters’ data mapping the Blackout’s effect zone, and Brunwyn’s data from interrogating the rogue Brackhaus, and Ringmaster’s data from his teams have been cutting old hardwires in the DDB, the team is finally able to trace the Blackout back to it’s origins, an old pre-Crash 2.0 wired matrix hub and power station, on Zug Island, in the river just SW of Downtown. SK offers to do recon and plan the assault, or let the team do their own recon and participate in the planning. However, the team will once again be the tip of the spear as SK is tied down holding Windsor against sporadic True Canadian terror attacks, and their forces in town are more brute force than infiltration specialists. Frau Brackhaus provides the team with hotsim rigs (for those who don’t have them) and gives Dig a copy of the “Hitchhiker” program, explaining they will likely have to dive in at some point.

Otters flies over in the VTOL with Morticia and bird Dig along. They do a recon pass and Dig spots a massive Plague spirit, but strangely no summoner, although Otters ID’s Mother Gorgon, the surviving toxie cult leader, as being on the island- they assume she must be masking. They also see the surviving Grid Pirates/Blackout Boyz, with heavy arms and armor, patrolling in two squads of 5. There is a hardened bunker-like building, a single crumbling causeway to approach on, and a collapsed dock on the far side of the island, so pretty good defensive position, but it also doesn’t look like they can retreat. The island is overgrown and has no obvious magical barriers- it seems like they were aiming to keep a low profile (which they have til now).

They plan the assault, with SK offering fire support from the WIndsor bank, while the team hits it from the Detroit side. SK will also attempt to manage any reinforcements that arrive and cut off any escape. They recruit Heat and Rose to help with matrix defense on the TacNet and figure some TM’s will be useful in general. Otters drops Creep off on the roof of the bunker with an alchemical token that Dig prepared, which starts shaping a hole in the concrete ceiling. Creep then catches sight of the huge patrolling spirit, which CG and Dig use to target their ritual- a massive lightning bolt which cooks off a bunch of the spirit’s scabrous armor. Creep starts blasting sensors on the bunker’s roof to mess with the defender’s weapons auto-tracking abilities.

PPJ goes astral to take on the spirit, as bird Dig and CG rejoin the fight, pouring fire on the spirit to try to finish it off so the van can approach. Otters gets some shots in as well with her new sniper drone, which does massive damage before being destroyed by acid spells from Mother Gorgon and the spirit. PPJ then successfully kills the spirit on the astral, and Otters starts racing the van up the causeway. Mother Gorgon goes astral as her remaining cultists drag her to cover, and Dig jumps in to astral as well to help PPJ against her.

With another brutal mage duel occurring in the astral plane, Creep and CG, using silenced weapons, pick off Mother Gorgons cultists from hiding, and then finally manage to kill her while she is away from her body. PPJ declares that he is best mage in the area as per usual. He returns to his body, and Otters flies him over to join the fight as CG keeps providing sniper support, Otters fires rockets and HMG rounds from the van, and SK mortar and sniper teams assault the remaining defenders now that the magical threat is dealt with.

They make short work of the surviving defenders on their side of the island, and SK reports they have the rest pinned down on the far side for now. CG uses his spirit’s movement powers to quickly dash down the causeway and join the team. Creep has finally snuck inside and gotten a look around- he spots Spritelok’s providing power, and shoots out the one locking the front doors. He also sees that the Blackout Boyz weren’t lying- many of their family members, including a number of children, are here, with a not insignificant number of weapons and what look like suicide vests.

In the van, Rose and Heat report that the TacNet is under assault from clear and red humanoid figures, the red ones drip worms from a writhing mass. Heat says he can hold it for now, while they task Rose with disabling the suicide vests. They hear her mumble “fuck em all” before she detonates them, killing everyone inside. When questioned she says she hates what the Grid Pirates made her in to but does not regret doing this. She is also badly injured from biofeedback. Not wanting to waste time (or ammo) CG has his spirits bash the door open and the team enters, with Heat staying behind to keep the van safe and block the entrance for anyone coming after.

They shut down the TacNet as the matrix assault increases and go LOS only. Wading through the carnage of the interior, they find another bunker-like inner chamber, air-gapped and still sealed, though the door is damaged. They bash their way in again, and Rose manages to get the elevator opposite working- going down. She takes even more damage doing so and is looking rough. She reports that the local matrix feels wrong and is filled with vines. They descend, and find a truly horrific site.

It seems that the plan here was to recreate “the 100” experiment- they only have about half as many TM’s but they are all hooked up to a massive central terminal. It looks a bit more jury-rigged than DelaMar’s facility under Boston, but likely still has some massive power for whatever it is they are attempting. Interestingly, there are no human technicians or attendants present- only drone manipulator arms on tracks. Worrying that time might be limited with the sound of renewed fighting above echoing down the elevator shaft, they approach the terminal and Dig hooks everyone up to their simrigs, the program reads all green, and they dive in.

They find that they have entered straight into the Foundation, and emerge into a dark carnival. Dangerously janky rides and hideous laughter abound. PPJ starts cutting some of the vines that grow everywhere, and instantly attracts attention from the white and black silhouettes wandering the area. Dig cuts him out of the Hitchhiker link, and PPJ gets dumped back into his body, deciding to focus on healing and monitoring the others. The rest of the team manages to remember to behave in a manner befitting the paradigm, and TD figures out the local layout. Otters is delighted to participate in carnival stuff and maybe has found a BTL addiction replacement in this hyper-real style of place.

They are also nearly overwhelmed by sporadic blasts of horrific noise. Otters retunes her RCC to bring the noise down to a manageable level, and they decide to track it back, figuring it must be important. Using TD’s understanding of the layout, they end up working their way up the line into the “tunnel of love” ride, which appears to be a river, clogged with vines, flowing downwards. The noise is coming from within. Upsettingly to all, it seems that not only are they in the Null Node, they appear to be able to travel down through it to something below- both Dig and Rose confirm that this is not a space known to exist.

With no other obvious options, they descend. Rose is almost instantly killed, and disappears, shattering into many tiny pieces. PPJ manages to stabilize her body in the basement as she seizes, but she seems changed. After assensing her, he realizes she is no longer a Technomancer! The rest of the team sneaks down the vines, spotting a grey silhouette at the bottom. They also appear to be back in the real world- they have their equipment again and are no longer having to fit with a paradigm. They open fire, and destroy the gray man, but this seems to attract further attention, as numerous of the Null Sect triangle mouths start flooding the chamber.

They spot a device in the center of the chamber, overgrown with vines, and labelled “Hengester SEED”. Otters opens fire on it and the others follow as the Null begin attacking them. Luckily Dig’s biofeedback filter absorbs much of the initial assault, but the team starts getting hurt, and PPJ frantically tries to stay on top of healing their bodies with the med drone and spells. Just as Creep finally blasts apart the device, he sees a huge switch on top, that based on vine growth patterns and such seems to have been turned on recently. Dig also was able to analyze the device a bit, and determined that it was the source of the noise, that it was hooked up to all the TM brains, that it was pulling energy somehow from the Resonance, and that this current project seemed intended to sever Resonance and Matrix connections.

With the device destroyed, the noise finally stops, but CG is quickly also killed alongside Dig as they fight the Null. PPJ manages to stabilize them both in their meat bodies. However, with Dig gone, and all the various shades of silhouette pouring into the chamber from the Foundation above as the vines retract, the rest of the team is one their own. Otters, tripping badly from some pyschotropic side effect of her wounds, notices some kind of strange pressure change, and TD and Creep are able to sneak through the crowd well enough to avoid further injury and guide Otters along with.

They head down the one side passage that none of the Null seem to be using, and find a rippling vortex blocking their way. With the Null closing in behind them, and seeing a glimpse of what looks like PPJ through a fisheye disttortion, they dive in, and all tumble back into their meat bodies- but it all felt so real! Only TD stays conscious from the dumpshock, though luckily PPJ is able to keep both the others from flat-lining. With the team all “safely” out of the matrix and badly injured, they prepare to figure out how to get out of there, hoping the fighting upstairs has wrapped up as they don’t have much capacity left.

They also see that all the TM’s hooked up to the terminal have flat-lined, and wonder if some of the ghostly forms they saw at the carnival were those same TM’s, slowly being used up…. They are able to ID most of the TM’s as ones reported missing around town since the Blackouts, but there are also a fair number of others, as if some were brought here ahead of time! Also- where were they? It seemed so real!

Spirited Competition
11/27/2023, 10:47:28 AM
Runners
Creep
Chew Gum
Dig
The Detective
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack

The remaining infiltration team, having barely escape the Black Lodge neighborhood before the ritual disruption caused a massive disaster, floats around in Lake St Clair for a while before Wood Raven finds them and hauls them back to shore, tactfully not mentioning the wreckage of his nice stealth speedboat. He tells them that a spirit dog showed up in front of him and guided him out to their floating wreckage raft, and Otters feels that Dog has perhaps repaid the favor owed to her.

When they get back to shore, Wood Raven helps Otters do some emergency drying out of all the drones and the team links back up with ChewGum and The Detective at the main offensive line/distraction attack position. TD had been helping with tactics and coordination. Creep also eventually shows up, a bit too late to assist, reporting that the spirits that attacked and chased him, trying to steal his leg, suddenly vanished.

Not getting any response on the radio, they decide to check up on the Belle Isle crew, and head that way, while Creep works on cracking the safe that Dig stole. Finding some explosives inside, he gets PPJ to use his spectral hand to disable them, and gets in. Creep is also a little weirded out, as is TD, by PPJ’s whole deal and backstory, especially given the revelation of there having been another PPJ in Black Lodge custody. At their insistence, though TD is much gentler about it, PPJ cuts himself and to his great relief, bleeds red.

Inside the safe are a number of Tarot cards and a sheaf of papers. The Tarot cards included 2 that the team hasn’t seen before related to the Seelie Court, one that was stolen from Smoach and Myrryr’s office, and one that was a component of the Crystal Bloodblade, which the BL has some historic interest in. There is also a mysterious 5th card, which seems to be from an entirely different Tarot. Creep stashes them for now, and finds that the mystery 5th card has some kind of repellent function with the standard 6th World Tarot cards, but manages to jam them all in his leg.

Looking at the sheaf of papers, they find it encoded using a language none of them are familiar with, though CG recognizes the use of ideograms, and PPJ spots some linguistic similarities to Sperethiel. TD is able to recognize some cryptographic qualities, and thinks it likely is using an ancient/obscure language as a cipher, rather than fully being written in alternate syntax etc.

PPJ checks the journal that he grabbed off Freddy Bales’ dad, who seems to have used the nom de guerre “Karyos”. The journal is also encoded, but has two other interesting features. One, there is one half of a key to the cipher in it. 2, it seems to have been magically linked to one or more other books, like a written version of Mindlink, but the spell seems to have recently collapsed.

Creep remembers that Penelope Beans was also in the BL sanctum basement, and suggests they check on her shop in Hamtramck after checking out Belle Isle. They arrive at the Isle to find it truly devastated. It seems the BL attackers were eventually fought off (likely unable to summon reinforcements given the team’s assault on Casa Aurelius), but at great cost. Most of the buildings are damaged or levelled, the Cleansing ritual has stopped, and a major portion of the populace has been killed or injured.

After using magic, drones, leadership, flexibility and strength to dig through the rubble, they manage to help pull out Rose and D2 (aka Daiyana Dimeeko) the sole survivors of the Belle Isle/Windsor crew. Rose seems embittered by her continual losses and a bit in shock. D2 is more pragmatic and explains that the BL was close to overrunning them so she was forced to set off some defensive demo charges she had placed around to protect the ritualists as long as she could.

She tells the team that the BL used some kind of horrifying new weapon that the survivors here are referring to as “ghost bombs”- some kind of alchemical container in which they had trapped dark spirits, including some of the ones that came through the rifts, during their various rituals and experiments. When opened or broken, the spirits madly attack anyone in their vicinity, which caused mass casualties and distress. The ritualists were also attacked in astral space by some massive conjured spirits, who luckily vanished right around the time the BL ritual went sideways in Casa Aurelius, so the team did still manage to help here, indirectly.

They eventually find a badly wounded Jaga recuperating inside a large congregation of plant life, and leave her to heal (with some guards), but Walker in Ways and most of the other ritualists have been slain. Dig offers to return to help more soon. Otters finds Howell killed, alongside his 2 cyber-tigers, and while talking to survivors TD and CG hear of a Vatican Sacred Detective who came to their aid, as well as one of the 36 Hidden Righteous Ones who awakened during the fight and managed to keep many civilians safe from the dark spirits, but unfortunately both of them were slain as well.

CG coordinates with The People of the Book to get their mobile crematorium and burial preparation team down here to help out, and Creep and Dig manage to make everyone uncomfortable by suggesting they call the ghouls to eat other remains, before TD smooths things over by clarifying that the ghouls will of course only eat BL remains, which everyone seems ok with. The team hauls the BL bodies off to one side and calls the ghouls. Then they head to Hamtramck.

Good thing they invested so heavily in the district! Local volunteer firefighters managed to quickly extinguish the massive blaze that broke in Penelope Beans’ shop and keep it from spreading. Investigating the burnt remains, they find evidence of alchemical arson, seemingly the same powerful fire magic that destroyed Smoach and Myrryr’s office. They find burnt reagents, tools, books and supplies, and a slagged anthrodrone covered in the melted remains of it’s realistic features “skin”.

Dig uses the catalog spell to search for anything else unusual, and finds a shovel head (the handle burnt) away from the other tools, as well as one loose floor tile. PPJ pries it up and digs a bit til he finds a book- it’s still warm from the extreme heat of the fire but this looks promising. TD goes to open it, carefully not exposing their skin, and barely avoids getting jabbed with a spring-loaded neuro-toxin needle. Looking carefully inside their seem to be no further traps, and this looks to be the other half of the cipher.

A number of pages were torn out and are missing, so there’s no further info than that, but the team can tell this was also part of the linked books spell that Karyos’ was also bound to. This one is also inert. TD hauls their open air bathtub up to the roof of the Tin Can since the team abandoned their safehouse out back, and they all head back to the Plat Zone to rest and work on cracking the code.

With TD and Creep’s skills and focus, and both halves of the key, they manage to make sense of the documents. It seems that the BL had initially conceptualized their Horror-summoning and binding plan based on some research done by something called “Project PILLAR”, run by the Ordo Maximus. The BL has apparently heavily infiltrated the OM and has good sources there, but seems concerned that the legendary vampire hunter Martin deVries may be onto Project PILLAR, so the OM set a trap for him, after being tipped off by the BL.

The BL doesn’t know how this went, as they heard no more from their spy in the OM, and then have lost comms due to the Blackout. The sheaf of papers also includes a lot of technical info about their rituals and plans, the ghost bombs, etc. The journal also proves interesting- it seems that everyone in the BL is constantly scheming. Karyos was worried that a subordinate wad trying to supplant him (the woman who lead the ritual where CG almost got sacrificed and Dog got split up), while also trying to supplant his own superior (Penelope Beans).

He complains that Beans has such a strong position due to her successful longterm infiltration of the IOND, which greatly upsets PPJ. He knew the Boston chapter of the IOND was compromised, but this implies a much larger corruption in his organization. Karyos knows that he needs to pull off something big to compete with Beans, so he made a deal with the Null Sect, through the rogue Brackhaus that the team recently exposed, to assist them with something called “Project IXION”. He apparently knew the Blackouts were coming, to some extent, and was able to prep for them.

He had already spent years building up the local BL presence, recruiting many to move to Detroit from DeeCee when the BL lost their power base there, feeling that ARES would be good protection against UCAS Shedim counterstrikes. He ramped this up even further in prep for the Blackouts, and then triggered his plan in the background while everyone else was distracted by bugs and no power. He also hints at a larger goal beyond his own/the BL’s power- humans need to be able to protect themselves properly against “deep/distant metaplanar threats” and this is how he sees to do that.

With all this disturbing paydata to mull over (and decide who to sell to), the team finally rests. The next day, they get a call from Ringmaster to come meet him and some other Plat Zone Irregulars in a large open lot not too far away. He warns them not to freak out as a portal opens and they hear a distant screaming. As a massive flaming buffalo head at the front of a biomechanical train emerges, they see another rift tear open and the train start entering that as it exits the other. It slows a bit as it moves until once “car” segment irises open and two figures inside frantically toss out a bunch of hardcases before leaping free themselves.

One of them semi-frantically asks if anyone has anything they can pay the train with to make sure it departs peacefully, and Creep offers up the Coin of Woe from his leg, since Cholula is no longer there to protest. The woman accepts it, and tosses it to another flaming buffalo head at the rear of the train as it prepares to scream- it instead chomps down on the Coin, and leaves peacefully, the rifts closing behind it one after the other. And thus also departs whatever remains of Iva Linus Hathaway.

The women uncloaks herself and reveals that she is Dr. Dyna Mite, the metaplanar travel researcher, who recognizes Creep, Dig, and CG. She seems a little horrified that they had a Coin of Woe, but explains it was actually perfect currency, as those trains consume souls to run- efficient travel, but costly and dangerous. She tells them a little more about other “ghost trains” which tend to run more regionally or along their old tracks, rather than free-roaming “Iron Buffalo”.

They chat a bit and catch up, and she explains that she had to leave Seattle, where they last saw her, saying that the Ordo Maximus was after her- she doesn’t know why exactly, all she was able to determine was that it had to do with something called “Project AVERNUS”. The team makes note. She also gives them the brief news update from outside- Free St Louis is official to go along with Seattle, the Sioux Nation has renamed themselves Oceti Sakowin after retaking a bunch of their ancestral lands, and they seems to be doing well in their new turf so far as they go the power back on quickly. Blackouts continue throughout the UCAS, with varying degrees of severity, but it seems like the Grid Pirates/Blackout Boyz haven’t been nearly as active anywhere outside of Detroit, though present in a number of cities.

As the other figure from the train uncloaks herself, she and Ringmaster engage in a handshake-to-hug, and the team recognizes Arleesh’s voice, Kendra de Santos. They all head back to the SCIF at the Plat Zone, and get into it. De Santos provides them with a bit more news from outside, and suggests that the BL ritual implosion that killed thousands of civilians in Casa Aurelius will be excellent PR to recruit for the Rogue Lodge, and then briefs the team on that a bit as well.

Ringmaster provides PPJ with some messages- Darien Black summons him to Denver, promising some information about his disappearance but not acting nearly as fatherly as PPJ always hopes, Jonny Silver survived the Wild Hunt and celebrated with a 100 hour set on a riverboat down to Free STL, and is now back in Denver running Perinawyr’s label and club, since P has been banned from Denver by Ghostwalker, and Prot0 is also apparently back in Denver, but in a secretive “international terrorist on the run” way. Guy is in the wind, presumably with Berto, and de Santos explains that Aylin now works, with her mother, for Grey Cell, and has been especially useful on anti-Fear the Dark and Ordo Maximus ops, being infected herself.

With this news confirming that PPJ will be headed to Denver alongside most of the team, de Santos suggests that it would also be a great town to recruit for the Rogue Lodge in, since they can’t operate as “openly” as usual given GW’s autocratic control. de Santos and Ringmaster next explains that as well as valuable medicines, reagents and the like that they brought on the train, they brought some more of the artifacts that the ASPS made in Chicago to establish a Spirit of the City there, and plan to do the same process here.

They hint that they aim to establish Detroit as a longterm R&D and training base for Grey Cell, as well as wanting it to stay free in general, which the team gets on board with.
