They say they will also need ritualists to stabilize and sustain the SOTC, esp as it first gets going, and Dig immediately volunteers to remain, distressing Creep, who is still reeling from Cholula’s loss, while also becoming closer and closer to cyberpsychosis himself. They decide to table it for now and talk about the job at hand.

Ringmaster provides them with a list of 7 likely candidates, though he says they are free to seek out more, and suggests they check them all out, and use their influence around town to bring different factions on board to help establish one as the new SOTC. With the Belle Isle cleansing ritual down, and the BL having fucked up local mana space significantly, this project should take some priority.

The team heads out to start their investigation, starting up near Hamtrack along 8 Mile at the Magic Bag theater. There, they interact with some Juggalos, who seem appreciative of the team’s efforts to get food and other supplies to town and distro’ed around, and introduce them to their spirit leader, Hatchetman. Dig stays in the van, refusing to say a single “woop woop”, even though Dog has some affinity for the callouts to “family”. The rest of the team spends a few hours there, and feels a beneficial sense of chaos, at least for the in group. The spirit seems to be engaged in voluntary karmic pacts with it’s followers, and seems focused on keeping them safe and well but also very willing to turn on outsiders.

They aren’t totally sold but other than Dig are not inherently opposed. Next they head to the Motown Museum in Downtown, and party with a mix of spirits and metahumans, who are rotating through various instruments, keeping a dance party going. The Museum seems impressively intact, and TD sees that some of The Keepers seem to use this as a safe downtown spot to sleep. They chat with Smokey, who seems to be “in charge”, and who explains that music is necessary for rebuilding, and people need to cut loose after all the trauma. They dance a bit, and Smokey even gets an earworm stuck in Dig’s mind, after he claims to never have cared about music.

Smokey seems to recognize the balance of business and art needed to make it work, so a strong contender currently. They head for the next one, at an old Ford plant turned museum in Dearborn. They find a bunch of gaunt but muscular workers laboring away to repair the surrounding area, under the guidance of an imperious spirit named “Henry”. They question him a bit, and he seems to have strong feeling about his paternalistic role towards his workers, and his vision for a city rebuilt for the “right” kind of people. Dig is totally onboard but most of the rest of the team is suspicious, especially given Ford’s political history.

They next head up to the Blight, to track down a spirit called simply “Blight”. They find some toxie cultists who call themselves the Scions of the Blight, hanging around the area known as the Rusted Heart. They take the team to a toxic pool, which bubbles and spews forth strange, rapidly growing and decaying vines and plant life, rapidly disassembling nearby structures. The spirit talks through it’s acolytes and calls for an end to Anthropocene- metahuman supremacy must be abolished, cities destroyed, and the world rewilded, through mutation, just another word for rapid change.

CG and Dig are entranced and want to sign on. They spend some team helping tear down some nearby infrastructure, while Dog feels a bit ambivalent and many of the others more so, though also participating for now. They head out but Dig is really onboard. They rest up after a long day before hitting up the remaining 3. The next day they start off with “Hatcher”, at the old Solidarity House in the Docklands. They find a mixed group of dock workers, downtown working class survivors, and Betas/Deso Angels hanging out, shooting the shit, drinking and organizing.

Behind the bar, Hatcher pours the team some drinks. Dig drinks a bunch extra when PPJ, Creep and Otters all refuse (2 for sobriety, 1 for no mouth). Hatcher seems pleased at Dig’s ability to toss them back but less impressed by his admitted lack of physical prowess. They chat for a while, and Hatcher comes across well- recognizing the need for employment and the ability to survive/make a living for the people of Detroit, but wanting to protect all the little people from corpo degradation. PPJ also picks up a buggy vibe in general, and realizes Hatcher may have multiple meanings.

Inquiring about bugs, Hatcher explains she’s against queens the same way she’s against bosses- she’s for collective organizing and power. They hang for a while and have a good time, before heading for the shores of Lake St. Claire, where they encounter Three Fires- 3 spirits who have sat themselves around a campfire, exchanging stories and gossip as a means of doing business, all seemingly having arrived via spectral canoes. These seem to represent the Council of 3 Fires, the Ojibwe, Ottawa and Potawatomi people whose ancestral homelands encompass the whole metro area.

They have a long and fruitful conversation, in which the spirits tell the team that especially with the news from out West, it is only a matter of time until all their lands are returned to them, but that the days of mass expulsions are over- assuming the UCAS doesn’t force their hands again. This seems to be more the long game vision though, and a Free Detroit seems reasonable in the meantime- always fun to stick it to DeeCee, plus the people here deserve better than either the Feds or ARES. The team chats and hangs for a while before leaving, also feeling these spirits are strong contenders.

Lastly, they track down Singing Sam, who seems to be glitching between classic Mafia attire and a more modern Triad look. They chat with him for a while and Dig manages to magically chill him out enough that he accepts the shift in local organized crime, taking on a Triad look fulltime. Dig also gets Smokey’s song stuck in Singing Sam’s head, who walks off whistling it with a purpose, seemingly reinvigorated. With all the info in, the team puts together a ranked choice ballot and votes. Hatcher wins out eventually, though the range of opinions was quite wide.

With this decided, they call on all the factional rep and contacts around town, distributing the ASPS artifacts around to everyone they can get onboard, and de Santos leads a ritual at the Plat Zone, setting up Hatcher as the SOTC. Hatcher’s first order of business is setting up a spirit council with the other contenders, all of whom accept except Henry, who prefers to go it along on executing his vision. The team hopes by not giving him more power, they staved off a “paper clip problem” situation, and are encouraged by Hatcher’s action, though Creep is disappointed not to have returned the control of the city directly to it’s Native people.

With that done, Brunwyn gets in touch and tells them that with Otters’ mapping data and the intel they managed to salvage from the rogue Brackhaus that the team revealed, she has managed to track down the “center” of the zap zone and presumably the source of the Blackouts- the old main power station from the wired grid on Zug Island, in the Detroit river. She tells the team it’s time to do some recon and plan their assault- they need to get the power back on.

The other big news is that with the SOTC chosen and initially established, the sky finally returns to Earth normal and the leylines around town start to normalize. Shortly after this occurs, SK troops who had held off a nearby Quebecois invasion attempt, enter Windsor in force and make short work of the “True Canadian” separatists there, focusing on lightning strikes and quick surrender once SK gains control of the airport. This puts them on a more even footing with Wuxing locally, given the heavy Triad influx, leaving AZT in the weak position of the corporate triad in town at the moment.

Turning the Tide
11/20/2023, 1:11:40 PM
Runners
Chew Gum
Dig
Otters aka Roadkill
and the mysterious return of: Pepper Jack

The team spends a few days healing, repairing armor, gear and drones, and resting. ChewGum composes another memorial poem for Cholula, which is quickly shared around the memorial boards around town, and posted at the Plat Zone alongside CG’s memorial to Locke. Condolences for Cholula’s death come in from various different avenues, though there is also some relief from those who are still concerned about Betas as a phenomenon. CG also spends some time in his ritual space and summons a detritus spirit, a new entity he has learned to engage with, which will hopefully disturb others less than the corpses he usually summons.

Ringmaster calls them over to the Plat Zone for a meeting, and explains a strange situation to them. He had teams out fighting leftover spirits and closing rifts all over Downtown, cleaning up after the team shut down the big BL ritual, and one of them encountered someone. He helped them fight off some spirits and close a rift, and said some stuff about Denver and some other things, while seeming very confused. Ringmaster brought him here and got him fed, but wants the team to do a debrief on him.

He brings them back into the SCIF room at the back of the Brickhouse, and they are surprised to see that the man sitting there looks to be none other than former team member Pepperjack, who they are all very familiar with from the team file, especially having recently reviewed some of PPJ’s notes on the Denver “reality rips” while fighting rifts here in Detroit. He does indeed seem quite confused, and Otters does careful etiquette work to try to bring him up to speed while introducing the team.

PPJ seems to really hate blood mages and initially has some trouble with Dig who explains Red magic somewhat poorly, insisting it is based on the power of blood. Otters explains that it’s not quite like that, and tries to explain the Tzuri group, which then leads into CG, who they have to convince PPJ is not a shedim. Luckily, once they explain that they are fighting the Black Lodge, PPJ agrees to assist. His memory has some major gaps, but he slowly recalls stuff from before his disappearance in Temple of Terror IV that checks out with the team file, so they believe it to truly be him.

PPJ refuses Dig’s offer to read his mind to try to restore missing memories, still not quite trusting him. They check in with Ringmaster and tell him that PPJ’s story checks out, and that he will be working with them for now. Ringmaster thanks him, and in payment for his assistance agrees to attempt to contact some of his former team members (Jonny Silver, Guy, Prot0, Aylin etc) when able, as well as get a message out immediately to Darien Black via TM comms.

Ringmaster explains that they need to round up every ally they can to hit the Black Lodge in their fortified wall neighborhood, before they can complete whatever it is that they’re attempting now. The team decides to host another shadow meeting at the Plat Zone, and splits up to contact everyone they think can help. PPJ is delighted to hear that one of his contacts, Erika Knowles, is in town, so he agrees to tag along to that meeting as well as a bunch of the other ones since he actually seems the closest to a “face” on the team at the moment, with The Detective still occupied in rebuilding Hamtramck.

Creep has also been off comms for a while, scouting out the BL defenses, but they leave him a message and hope he checks it. CG and PPJ head up to Hamtramck and give TD a brief on the plan, as well as Sikh Burn and their other allies. They agree to send what help they can, with Sikh Burn planning to attend the meeting to represent their area. They stop in at Penelope Beans’ shop to get PPJ stocked up on reagents, as he reappeared with very little equipment and no money.

PPJ is instantly weirded out by Beans, who he has actually heard of before- she was listed as the info broker/supply point of contact for IOND shadow ops in Detroit when PPJ was doing that work for Darien Black back in Denver. She asks him about his affinity for crystals suggestively and he deflects, which also weirds CG out a bit. CG starts to notice that there seems to be a tiny lag in Beans’ responses, and they quickly assense her, finding no aura at all. Not having time to investigate further, they tag this to look into, buy their reagents, and head out.

Back at the Plat Zone, the team starts questioning PPJ about some of the things that Beans said about him, and he reveals that he has a blood Crystal spine, but refuses to acknowledge that it is blood magic at Dig’s consternation. When Otters scans him more thoroughly, she finds another small implanted crystal alongside the spine, which PPJ has no memory or knowledge of. He now agrees to let Dig read his mind, and Dig goes all out, finding that there is a large blank period between his disappearance in Boston and reappearance here, though it is only about 1/10th the amount of time that passed on Earth, implying some metaplanar travel and time distortion.

PPJ has only faint memories of a mazelike city, and being kept there against his will and forced to do things, and Dig is able to get a little more info there, and recognizes the memories of the city as “Dis”, that chaotic city that the team had caught a brief glimpse of, and managed to get a Quicksilver plate of, on the moon, through an anomalous portal there. But what does it mean? Dig analyzes the mystery crystal further, as it seems connected to PPJ’s brainstem, and tries to detect thoughts on it. It seems alien in nature- he’s unfamiliar with it as either a tech device or a magical spell/construct and can’t fully make heads or tails of it, but can tell it is storing something.

Also it seems to be made of the same material as the manatech weapon chips that TM’s have been digging for around the Scar blast zone. Dig decides to Mind Probe the crystal as well, and finds that it is storing some of PPJ’s missing memories, somehow, as well as working similarly to a Blood Crystal, in terms of providing an implant-like function in exchange for essence that would be lost if it were removed. He sees PPJ fighting and performing surgeries (making Chimeras, similar to the ones encountered at Site 000), all seemingly not under his own control, or in a kind of daze.

A cloaked figure eventually takes PPJ away after quickly implanting this crystal, and leads him on a mad flight through the chaotic streets of Dis, before shoving him through a portal, leading him into what looks like Kadath. PPJ stumbles through Kadath for a while before tumbling out into Detroit, and being found by Ringmaster’s crew. PPJ, with some memories restored, compares notes with the team on how terrifying Kadath and the Bone Bridges beyond are, and is disturbed when Otters seems to be a little too interested in Bridge building.

Otters also insists on deeper scans, and runs a comparison with Chimera samples they got for Knowles in Site 000, and finds that PPJ has been altered. She then does a thorough radar scan, and finds the alteration- an extremely long tongue that wraps around the interior of PPJ’s skull, like a woodpecker. However, it seems more lizard-like when Otters yanks it out to full extension and runs a series of experiments. It seems to extend very rapidly to a 3-4 meter range, and be both prehensile and very sticky.

PPJ goes along with this, but then, disturbed, leans on a wall, and is horrified when his hand phases through. He tells the team he can feel inside the wall and seems upset so they yank him out, pulling some drywall with. After some experimentation, he finds he can make his hands spectral, and slide them through solid surfaces, though it doesn’t work on objects and he can’t bring stuff back through. The team, thoroughly creeped out, takes notes, but it seems like since PPJ is as surprised as them, they stick with him for now.

They round up a number more allies from all over town, and get ready for the big meeting. Everyone shows, with the exception of Creep and Jaga/Walker in Ways. After some deal-making, overseen by Ringmaster, it shakes out as follows: The Betas will get to keep former ARES HQ tower, now renamed Freehive, as a base for their new style of merge, the University will remain a free enclave, Hamtramck assures it’s ongoing semi-independence, the gang coop assures it’s dominance of local street level crime (with a pro-social aspect), Biyu lays claim to syndicate control of Detroit via the Triad, with the ports under Wuxing’s aegis, and SK and AZT makes plans to split up the rest of the town, seeming to recognize that a somewhat freeform area can be useful to both their corps, with appropriate controls in place.

Otters makes the case for the streets as best she can after Ringmaster pushes a bit, and the corporate triad agrees- Brunwyn makes comparisons to Berlin having it’s uses, Cehualli seems happy to have space for some black labs and also an opportunity to make ARES look bad on a massive PR scale, and Biyu seems quite thrilled with holding onto everything she managed to grab during the crises here. Ringmaster side channels the team to tell them that he has some plans in the works he will bring them further into later also, to help ensure the city stays free.

With the meeting concluded and everyone onboard, Brunwyn privately insists on 2 more personal conditions for her assistance- PPJ will be interviewed by an SK doctor once Detroit reopens, and the team will assist her with getting the matrix issues resolved. She compares notes quickly with Otters and thinks that together they should be able to finally determine the origin of the Zap Zone. She explains that the rogue Brackhaus the team helped take out a few days back had apparently been working for the Null Sect inside the corp, having been lured into their employ with promises of treatment for his CFD infection, which he kept secret from the corp for fear of the obvious repercussions.

The team agrees, and Brunwyn signs on fully as well. The team gets a panicked message from Creep, saying he couldn’t make the meeting as he got ambushed by some people who tried to steal his leg. He got away for now but needs to keep running/hiding, and go dark while he does. They also haven’t heard from Jaga and Walker in Ways still, so bird Dig and Otters in a VTOL zip over to Belle Isle, and find it under attack both physically, from a few dozen well-armed and armored BL soldiers, as well as on the astral, where some powerful spirits are assaulting the cleansing ritual.

Both Dig and Otters are quickly spotted, though it seems the battle is pitched enough that they make their escape without coming under attack. Returning to the Plat Zone and reporting in, they all decide that they need to launch the attack on Casa Aurelius immediately, with at least some BL forces elsewhere engaged, and hope that splitting the BL’s attention can help their allies on Belle Isle. Wood Raven comes through with a stealthed rocket speedboat for the team to use to insert via Lake St. Clair, and Patricia Loveland (finally meeting in person now that Cholula is dead, though she still distrusts the Betas), Erika Knowles, and Ellis manage to make some quick and dirty mini-portal closers for the team to distribute around the area once they sneak in.

Everyone else will assault Casa Aurelius’ defenses from the land side, hopefully distracting the defenders and allowing the team to pull off their risky alpha strike. They get a number of side messages offering big paydays in exchange for paydata and items recovered from the BL stronghold. The team drives to the waterfront east of the Bligh, and loads up into the boat. Otters steers it down, remaining out of sight of guards on the shore. PPJ spots some patrolling spirits, and they make a plan. Otters, with her new sniper drone, and CG with his rifle, both take down one of the two guards on the beach they choose for their landing.

PPJ hits one of them again with a spell to keep him down, while Dig uses magic hands to pull them both into the water until they drown, while also hopefully concealing any TacNet signals. They approach, with Otters accidentally beaching the boat on some tank traps, but not catastrophically. Dig uses magic hands again to lift up the concertina wire strung along the shore, careful not the break the circuit flowing through it, and the team sneaks up to the mana barrier in between spirit patrols.

Dig reshapes his aura to look like Freddy Bales, and easily slips through. He then assists the others as they power down their various magics and enter, and has en easy time bringing all the drones through as he has “legitimate access”. The team sneaks through town, seeing the battle kicking off on the land side, as well as hearing distant muffled sounds of fighting ongoing from Belle Isle. Otters begins planting their mini-portal closer bombs via stealth drone at magical junction points spotted by the mages, as they all conceal themselves from foot patrols. One of which comes by seeking Freddy Bales- apparently someone detected “his” entrance through the barrier.

Dig does some quick bird recon, and finds Bales’ manor, figuring it’s a good target for looting. CG finds a sniper perch to keep a lookout and cause a distraction if needed, and Otters keep planting charges. Dig and PPJ head to the manor, and PPJ pulses the front to shut down electronic defenses, using his spectral hand to unlock the deadbolt from inside the door. They enter, and poke around, finding a study, where Dig uncovers a safe set in the floor, which he magically shapes concrete to free and then lifts out with his magic hands. PPJ reaches around inside but can’t open it, so they bring it with for now.

Otters has most of the charges planted when CG spots some other BL snipers taking high vantage points in preparation for the area’s defense. He takes them out with his rifle, but then has to flee, getting tagged a couple times by guards as he runs away. He leads them on a chase for a bit, allowing Otters time to plant the last few charges, before CG runs out of the barrier towards town, fleeing to the safety of his allies’ assault lines. He gets hit a few more times but luckily is defended by his spirit and mostly takes them on the armor.

Meanwhile, PPJ and Dig have found a secret door down to a sound-proofed and well-appointed basement. Sneaking forward, they find Bales’ dad in conversation with none other than Penelope Beans herself! Both appear to be BL leaders, and strangest of all, they have another PPJ tied to a chair in a vault room in the basement. They get in a magical fight, managing to take out Bales’ dad as Beans tries to seal the vault door. Dig prevents it with magic hands as PPJ quickly grabs a journal that slid out of Bales’ dad’s robe when he collapsed to the floor and died.

They get in a brutal magical slugfest with Beans, who deals them both a lot of damage before Dig finally manages to smack her with his magic hand, distracting her. Otters, searching for them now that the charges are planted, finally locates the basement, and rushes to their aid, blasting Beans with a Mule drone, killing her. Otters says they need to get out of there, and heads back upstairs, hearing some guards approaching, reporting 2 “high value assets’” biomonitors just triggered.

Otters waits for an opportune moment, and then detonates the charges outside to distract the response team, before fleeing out the back, telling the others to follow. In the basement, PPJ is befuddled by his seeming exact double, and the double (drugged and slurring) keeps insisting that he is the real one, and that the false one will bleed purple. Dig gives in and cuts the double, who seems completely horrified to see purple blood pour from the wound.

As PPJ tries to get them to leave and figure it out later, Dig fires the double with a lightning bolt, and then flees. The team races away from the back of the mansion as pressure builds in the air, heading towards the boat. Dig manages to hoist it off the tank traps partway with magic hands and Otters rocket boosts the craft free, though it is holed and taking on water. CG keeps shooting on the town side as the mana barrier starts to crackle and destabilize. Vihn Trong and other mages race forward to contain the expected blowback as best they can, as Otters races the boat straight away out into the lake.

As the district implodes inside the barrier and then explodes out from it in a massive pressure wave, the mages are able to deflect it out into the lake. The boat is flipped, but Otters in the VTOL and bird Dig manage to fly through it, and then are able to make enough of a drone raft to hoist PPJ up and keep him from drowning. They are eventually rescued by some allies after a while floating, during which they watch the astral sky stabilize to a more normal appearance. Not quite regular night stars yet but getting closer!

Rifts vs Riffs
11/13/2023, 10:06:38 AM
Runners
Creep
Chew Gum
Dig
Otters aka Roadkill
Tabasco aka Cholula

Dig starts off the day by heading up to his magical order meeting at the zoo. Everyone trickles in, with Freddy Bales there before even Dig, and his usual preppy self, and then the other 2 showing up, concealed (Norman Burger) or wrapped up in defensive magic (Marjo-Lin). Dig gives an inspiring introduction to the day’s lesson plan, and Bales gives him a subtle thumbs up, casually undermining his authority as per usual. The initiation goes well however, and everyone succeeds under Dig’s guidance, gaining access to new powers and abilities.

Dig heads back, and the team briefs with Ringmaster- the rifts are still popping up, and still need to get looked into- he’s trying to keep anyone he can off the Downtown streets, whether that’s getting them out of the district or underground (where there don’t seem to have been any rifts yet). They head out and Creep and Otters work up a rift map of the known occurrences thus far, and start trying to guess where others might be based in their estimation of the cause/origin in the portal weapon that Vogel detonated to end the bug threat.

Dig casts a massive catalog spell to seek the strange reflective black metal, and they quickly find a rift. Two large horned apes in heavy smoking armor wielding greataxes have cornered a group of civilians and already taken some heads. The team decides they have to get involved, and CG debuts his new astral combat spell which he just learned from Dig earlier in preparation. They manage to send the two spirits back through the rift and get a drone in just in time to get some sensor data before it snaps shut. Dig also gets some magical analysis done. Unfortunately, the van takes some heavy damage from one of the axes.

The drone through the rift saw that the spirits, after being disrupted in our plane, seem to have reformed in Kadath, not healed but healing, so probably for the best that the team didn’t follow them in. Otters once again sees “through” Kadath to a Bone Bridge, but perceiving it through drone sensors seems to blunt the impact to some degree. Dig manages to determine that the rifts seems to be temporarily reaspecting their immediate area into effectively being a part of Kadath, which is probably why most of the spirits stick pretty close to them.

He also sees some magical tendrils drifting away from the rift towards the area of Downtown under the Scar, which looks unusually turbulent today. Otters and Creep have also compared some notes while updating their rifts map, and determined that they have a centerpoint- the former plaza/park that was right out front of ARES HQ downtown, next to the site of the detonation that caused the Scar. They head that way, stealthily, to see what’s up.

They manage some good recon without getting spotted, but what they see is pretty awful- a circle of 12 Black Lodge mages have a massive magical dome up around their ritual circle, and seem to be pulling some kind of cyclonic astral tunnel down from the Scar, though it seems to travel through and beyond Kadath now, as if they have redirected that astral effect. It hasn’t touched down yet, and seems to progress in jerks and starts, likely as more rifts open, from what they can tell.

They also have 4 heavy gunners set up in well defended positions with good views watching for any mundane threats approaching, and 2 adept guards with mage blades fighting off any spirits. Interestingly, the strange spirits that have emerged from the rifts seem drawn to this place, so they have their hands busy, acting as a quick response force to keep them from reaching the ritual dome.

The team tries to call it in to Ringmaster, and finds that either the magic or a jamming field is blocking comms, so they retreat a bit to call it in. Feeling that the threat level warrants it, they also decide to try to use their last two major arcana. Dig easily activates The Eclipse card, and sees a horrifying vision of the future if the ritual succeeds, which seems hyperreal to him, but is undetectable to the others. It shows the cyclone touching down, and the BL using this ritual plus others around town to fragment and capture a Horror from beyond the Chasm, across the Bone Bridge, chaining it with incantations and bindings as they slice it apart to make it manageable.

As this occurs, Dig sees in the vision, the local astral plane is immediately reshaped and made terrible, almost like an inverse of the establishment of a Spirit of the City, and much faster. Dig gets the feeling that even though they seem barely able to hold the containment, this will grant the BL an almost unimagineable amount of power, at least locally. He emerges from the vision trance to find the card gone, with no time having passed for the others. He describes what he saw, and they make the call as Creep tries to puzzle out the Discipline card.

Ringmaster instructs them to attack as they likely can’t afford to wait, and says he will call for aid but needs the team to slow or stop the ritualists in the mean time as it will take some time to muster assistance. Creep finally chills out enough, with CG’s assistance, to center himself, and lay out and clean all his gear in prep for this upcoming monumental task, and succeeds in activating Discipline. It manifests as a powerful Task spirit, in the guise of Rosanne era John Goodman, who says he will assist Creep and protect him during his upcoming ordeal. A drone zips in close to the van and a prerecorded message from Wood Raven wishes them luck, dropping off two missiles to get revenge for all the Swarm members lost to spirits and rifts lately.

Feeling as prepped as they can and with no time to waste, they make a quick plan of attack and head back to the ritual area. Creep and Cholula approach through the DDB tunnels, planning to attack from the East, where the final big bug breakout left a large opening, while the rest of the team hits from the SW. They set their mission timers, and wait to hit “go” until they see some strange spirits on the approach to distract the mageblade guards.

As some horrifying birdmen (who seem to perhaps have been lured here by small figures who slip away) attack, the team makes their move. Otters hits the spirits and mageblades with simultaneous missile and overlapping fields of fire from the two Mules, which takes out the mageblades and damages the spirits. The team finds the other 4 guards quite a bit harder, and gets into a brutal shootout with them, with Dig launching magical attacks from the cover of the van, CG sniping from extreme range at height, Otters firing from the van and two drones, and Creep and Cholula making their approach and hitting the opposite corner guard with flaming arrows, elephant guns and spirit powers.

The guards, heavily armed and armored, make fairly short work of both Mules and the van, spinning up their miniguns with devastating effects. Creep almost takes a couple bad hits that John Goodman saves him from, and Cholula gets tagged a couple times. Luckily, between Cholula’s flaming arrows and the spirit’s accident power, they manage to detonate their guards gun, and are able to chip away at his armor, even while coming under fire from one of the remaining guards.

CG has range on the guards, whose high rate of fire miniguns can’t reach his sniper perch, so he contents himself to just pick away at their armor when he can get a shot in, and also provide counterspelling support in case the ritualists get involved, which half of them quickly do. They turn to face outwards, and somehow channel some of the ritual energy into the 3 attacking birdman spirits, seemingly sucking their essence into the ritual and increasing it’s power and stability via their sacrifice.

Otters sneaks out of the van, staying in cover, and starts piloting the snake drones into the combat area to attempt some sneak attacks. Dig slips out in animal form and keeps launching spells. They gradually whittle away the guards, eventually finishing them off, but unfortunately have come to the attention of the ritualists, who have begun to target them with that same energy draining effect that destroyed the spirits. Luckily, with the guards gone, they are relatively free to maneuver so Creep gets John Goodman to massively increase his speed and plants all the team’s explosives around the magical barrier, detonating them and cracking it.

As the mages flip between targets, Cholula is badly injured and forced to retreat into the tunnel, while Creep is nearly killed, though John Goodman saves him, pushing him out of sight and getting destroyed in his place. Dig and CG are both targeted and injured, though they manage to stay in the fight with their counter-spelling. They all pour fire onto the damaged magical dome, including Cholula arcing explosive arrows out of cover, as Otters, badly injured herself from dumpshock, crawls forward to grab one of the Mule’s guns, as a distraction, before going prone and hot into the snakes.

They manage to crack the dome, destroying the mage’s magical protection, and begin attacking directly. CG starts blasting them with the sniper rifle, while also telling Cholula to reemerge from his hiding place to attack as well, he quickly lights some on fire. With all this distraction, Otters manages to successfully KO a couple mages with the snake drones’ darts- when they lose focus, they themselves are sucked into the ritual and destroyed. It seems to become more chaotic and less stable with each mage lost, but the team doesn’t have a better plan and only intermittent comms so they continue.

Dig is forced to hide himself behind a summoned concrete shield to break line of site as he is almost killed, and then turns to a bug and sneaks into the ritual circle while maintaining his concrete manipulation spell. CG covers him with sniping and counterspelling as best he can. Cholula is badly injured and nearly killed by the mages, who also finally manage to destroy the snake drones with lightning bolts, also nearly killing Otters from the dumpshock. As CG watches his team fall in horror through his scope from far away, Dig makes a last desperate move, pulling on his reserves of good fortune to shape concrete in such a way as to disrupt the ritual circle and it’s runes and markings.

With all the mages still alive and focused, they might have stood a chance, but as is they lose control. As they start getting hit with the massive drain of the failed ritual, and Dig flees the side effects, the BL mages begin bursting and being sucked into the ritual. The final mage throws back his hood, and shouts a horrified “teach! no!” revealing himself to be Freddy Bales, before slapping his hands together and himself exploding. He seems to have been the ritual leader, and the team sees that although the astral cyclone withdraws without ever having made touchdown, he was able in his final moment to redirect much of the energy of the ritual and it’s many sacrifices somewhere to the East.

Unfortunately, they also see that being the last one the ritual was tied to, Cholula has also been destroyed and sucked in, his limited essence and force providing some further power to some dark purpose as he is annihilated. Having nothing left of him to even try to heal, Dig and CG hustle to stabilize and heal Creep and Otters, and begin collecting drone and van parts they can salvage, as the reinforcements finally arrive. Brunwyn sent along her personal guards, who tell the team they are sorry they missed the fight (sincerely) but suggest a meeting with their master soon. A variety of other runners and Plat Zone regulars also show up, and secure the scene and provide support and transport to the team.

Dig invents a new drink called “The Cholula” and everyone takes shots of it back at the Plat Zone. Creep gets a new cyberarm to try to buff up his failing body a bit- it’s barely even surgery since he has the quick release ports. Dig is in a weird mental state, seeing as he lost a friend and also a student betrayed him, so he sentimentally holds onto Creep’s meat arm (literally), planning to keep it in the Vault of Ages. The team debriefs with Ringmaster, as CG does some scouting to the East to see where all that mana went. Upsettingly, it seems that the walled Casa Aurelius district is now covered in a fully opaque magical barrier, with armed guards patrolling outside.

The team remembers that Freddy Bales introduced them to an older man who he identified as his father at the museum gala in that district, and are horrified to confront the fact that although they were able to halt the main ritual, the threat of the Black Lodge may not yet be over. Passed on CG’s and other scouts’ reports, Ringmaster says they likely have a little time to heal, re-equip, and muster allies, since the BL seems to be on a defensive footing, but that they shouldn’t delay an assault for too long, as who knows what kind of nefarious deeds the BL might get up to in the meantime.

However, some good has come of the team’s actions already- they prevented the primary ritual, and this seems to have closed the Scar, as well as ending the sporadic rifts opening around town. None have been sighted since the ritual was disrupted, and Ringmaster has teams out cleaning up any straggler spirits and making sure their associated rifts close. The spirits seem to all be from the deep metaplanes or beyond- some are what are often classified as “lesser Horrors” or “Tzitzimine.”

The team rests and heals for a few days, with Otters, in a chipper mood as always, sharing memories of Cholula with those who will listen. Marjo-Lin tells Dig that she and some other Keepers were the ones that lured the birdman spirits over to the ritual, having heard Ringmasters call for aid, and also tells Dig she’s sorry for what happened to his magical order- apparently Norman Burger was another one of the BL ritualists! ML tells Dig that she was a bit surprised as she never got a human supremacist vibe off of either one, but this does explain Bales’ skill as masking his aura and appearing innocuous.

Dig briefly considers killing ML as well, just in case, but Otters talks him out of it. For one, she’s an ork, making her very unlikely to be a Black Lodge member. Also, she’s one of The Detective’s close contacts in the Keepers. For now, the team rebuilds and begins to try to figure out what to do about Casa Aurelius- a rich human-centric district makes a lot of sense as a BL stronghold.

Corporate Confusion and Dodging Rifts
11/6/2023, 12:12:41 PM
Runners
Creep
Chew Gum
Dig
Otters aka Roadkill
Tabasco aka Cholula

While resting and repairing at the new Plat Zone safehouse, the team gets a few messages. Peregrine says that he has a Johnson who specifically requested their team for a job, and Ringmaster says he needs some recon done around town on some rumors of small rifts appearing and vanishing ever since the Black Lodge ritual got disrupted and imploded. Creep pushes hard for doing the Peregrine gig for some solid non-magical payday times, and the team eventually agrees, though Cholula and Otters hope to maybe encounter some rifts on the way to get some quick intel.

They head out towards The Dom enclave, and only make it halfway before encountering a rift- 3 strange corrupted spider hybrids with long spindly legs are dragging a pile of drugged and bound captives back into a rift. CG sets up the quicksilver camera while the rest of the team engages the spider’s in combat, with Otters firing the van gun while Cholula charges in. He spots that the spiders have bound the captives with some sort of magical webbing after disabling them with a toxin, which he almost falls prey to himself.

They manage to defeat the spiders, and save the final captive as the rift puckers shut with an audible snap. Doing some investigation onsite, they are able to find a small fragment of unidentifiable black reflective stone, and crumbled and burnt remains of some more of the stuff. This seems to be what AZT mentioned the TM’s have been digging for, and doing some quick CSI, Otters is able to determine that the magitech weapon that detonated over Downtown, creating the Scar, could have shot shrapnel out to this locaiton.

Dig is able to detect some magical traces, that suggest that this tiny fragment was part of a larger whole, powered by sacrificial magic some time previous, and to do with portals or gateways. It seems to still have a small amount of power onboard so they stash it in Creep’s leg, and head back to the Plat Zone, with CG using his medkit to stabilize their rescued victim. At the Plat Zone, Dig asks if there are any TM’s around, and Electric Blue comes forward, and volunteers to touch the weird fragment at Dig’s request.

He says he feels a little more connected and whole, so Dig checks him out in the astral, and sees that he seems to have a possibly temporarily enhanced essence, but that this has drained the small remaining power from the fragment. It doesn’t seem to be instantly addictive, but EB does his best to describe the feeling, and how he can understand the pull of this would have, especially to anyone who had lost some of their essence to injury, implant, etc and wanted to restore that connection.

Ringmaster seems worried about this development, and even more worried about what the team tells him they briefly glimpsed through the rift, which he confirms with a look at the quicksilver plate. It opened into the infinite maze of Kadath, and though only Otters say through and beyond, her report of seeing the construction of a Bone Bridge is upsetting, Ringmaster warns her to guard her mind and dreams, but since she lacks the ability to reach the astral plane herself, thinks she should be reasonably safe for now. He requests more info, as they team continues their travels around town.

Continuing on their way, they reach the bar adjacent to the bank that is now included in the expanding Dom “safe zone”. They enter the bar, and find one of the Brackhauses from the hotel mission awaiting them with their bodyguards. Now appearing with a feminine aspect, and unclear which was the “true” appearance if either, the team decides to refer to this one as “Frau Brackhause” for expediency. The young Vietnamese street mage and Black troll bodyguard are keeping an eye on the meet from different points in the bar, but give the team a nod of recognition from their last encounter in the DDB.

Sitting down, Frau Brackhaus gives them the rundown- she wants to sabotage the other Brackhaus (who the team decided to refer to as “Herr Brackhaus” for expediency). She tells them that she will make sure their team is recommended to him for a job he is putting together, and that all she wants them to do is record the meet and the job, leaving themselves out of the footage, and plant a program in a system they will access as part of the job. She gives them a chip with the program on it, and asks if they want to negotiate.

Lacking the skill, Otters requests “a little more money than your initial offer”, and she chuckles and tells them what she’ll pay. They agree, head back out to the van, and shortly receive a radio call from Herr Brackhaus, requesting a meet, in a short while at the same bar. They agree, and Otters circles the block until it’s time to head back in. They find Herr Brackhaus waiting at a different booth, but with his 2 twin adept bodyguards similarly deployed keeping an eye on things. They seem a bit less friendly to the team.

HB explains that he wants the team to break into an electronics store and swap out some data cores, and then break into an apartment and steal any electronics present and fence them at the Trading Post, where he set up an account that will deliver their pay. The store break-in must be unnoticed and the apartment must be somewhat obvious. If this all goes well, he requests they come meet him after at the bar atop the hotel where they first met.

Dig inserts the program that Frau Brackhaus gave them into one of the data cores, and they decide not to investigate either that or the cores themselves. They reach the address of the store in the Golden Mile shopping district along the west riverfront, and do some quick recon. The lone proprietor seems to be running in and out of the store doing sales outfront and inventory inside, while a guard watches the front. It’s in a recently converted Stuffer Shack, so all the disused vents and grease traps are still onsite but not running.

There’s an emergency exit on the alley at the back, and no running security cameras, so they go for an old school distraction out front while other sneak in. Cholula and CG feign getting into a fight while Dig does some magical mood manipulation, and Creep sneaks in a vent and opens the alley door for one of the Mule drones, which Otters rigs in and uses to swap the 3 data cores. The distraction works, but the market seems to have some magical security as a guard comes running warning everyone of magical manipulation. Cholula tries to hip check him and gets whacked, hard, with a stun baton for his trouble.

Dig becomes visible, announces he is the magical mastermind, and then turns into a bird and flies off. With the guard a bit distracted by this, Cholula and CG take this opportunity to flee, with all 3 feeling they may not want to return to the market for a while. They head to apartment next, luckily encountering no more rifts on their drive across Downtown. They start doing recon on the apartment, and are hyper-suspicious of the fact that it seems to be a regular nice condo in a guarded building without any traps or special security.

Cholula and Creep sneakily scale up to the unit while Dig watches for ambushes and guard patrols, and CG and Otters drive back to the Plat Zone to pick up the VTOLs to load the goods. On their way back, they spot some horrifying floating heads with dangling tentacles chasing and capturing some children, and decide to ignore it, feeling underpowered. They both take a hit with their rep with the Keepers in response. They get the VTOL’s and head back, returning to the team with no further trouble, having also reported in their new sighting to Ringmaster.

The team executes a simple B&E with no issues and gets a good haul of high end consumer electronics, as well as a monogrammed shaving kit, though it doesn’t appear to have been used and has no ritual samples. They head to the Trading Post, sell the goods, and get paid as promised. They head to the Dom again and call Frau Brackhaus on the mesh network. FB thanks them for the data they got, pays them, and tells them to do the same thing again for whatever job HB offers them next (record, report in, get paid twice). They agree and head back to the hotel.

They spot the floating tentacle heads again, this time with no kids, but looking a bit inflated and moving slower. Otters takes a pot shot but they seem especially resistant to physical weapons, so she speeds away. They drive off and call it in to the Plat Zone on the radio. At the hotel, realizing that some of them had gotten spotted during their attack there, only CG and Otters head in, getting a pat down but not an especially hard time from the concierge. They reach the bar up top, and find the troll crooner, matronly info broker and other regulars ensconsed as usual, with some patrons drinking by the windows and calling out when they spot new rifts popping up around Downtown.

They head to HB’s corner booth and slide past his alert twin guards. He kicks them over a heavy armored case, and explains that he has a ton of corp scrip that he needs to swap for liquid assets, via a Triad exchange, and also use this to buy a short term Triad safehouse. Once the team makes the swap, returns with spendable currencies, and gets the safehouse set up, he’ll have one last task for them, and pay double of the last job. They agree, grab the case and head out. They encounter another rift near the scar, with a huge mass of flesh, eyes, and mouths chasing and absorbing some survivors, and growing as it does.

As Otters prepares to fire, it foes invisible, but Dig and Cholula confirm it is still around, so the team opens up as best they can. It moves upsettingly quickly once attacked, and gloms onto the van, beginning to absorb Cholula. Luckliy Dig is able to do some magical damage, and Otters uses a slick van maneuver to break it’s hold and throw it off, after which it retreats to its rift. Dig almost follows it in, but is talked out of it just as the rift snaps shut, with some of the team vowing not to stick around if he does. Both Dig and Cholula really want to enter a rift though!

After some discussion, Dig flies ahead to give Grandmother Biyu a respectful heads up about the double Johnson situation on this job so she doesn’t waste a good safehouse, and she has them meet with her semi-captive TM Tater, who seems to still be working off his debt. They make the cash exchange at truly extortionate rates, and get the safehouse address and details. Otters, fearing more rift encounters, decides to solo VTOL back to the bar, and uses the rooftop bar since she was already screened for entry, though she is patted down once more.

She passes over the much lighter case, and passes along the address. HB then gives them the final task- a hit on the local SK middle manager who is overall in charge or the corp’s ops here during the Blackout. HB explains that he has personal beef with this manager, and that instead of seeing HB’s promise and advancing him, the manager has been wasting both of their time playing HB off against another local Brackhaus while he claims all the credit for their successes. Otters asks a few questions but agrees- it seems this middle manager is the guy whose monogrammed shaving kit they found earlier. HB is able to provide them with some ritual samples, however.

Otters zips over to the Dom and back to the Plat Zone, getting approval to continue from FB, who seems quite content with how this is all going. All she wants is for them to tell her first when the hit is done. Otters tries to make sure the team isn’t going to get any blowback, and FB suggests that so long as they did their job and aren’t on the recordings, there shouldn’t be any issues. Dig uses the ritual samples to track the guy down, after which the team decides that the easiest option is just to have Dig do the hit too, since he can mask his magic.

He flies to the manager’s location as a bird, astrally disguises his aura to look like a rift and casts an illusion of a rift crackling with lightning, and the bursts the manager with a lighting bolt. Hearing some panicked screams from witnesses and passers-by about how “oh god the rifts shoot lightning now” and similar, he flees the area. With that done, Otters VTOLs back to the Dom, gets paid, and gets a “well done” from FB, who explains that as the survivor of this whole situation she will have greater discretionary budget available soon, and likely more use for the team.

They decide not to report in to HB, not wanting to get in whatever trap FB has set for him, and skip the final chunk of their pay. Dig muses about how cheap it is for someone to get the team to kill someone, especially when they don’t even bother to pick up the money. They rest up as CG initiates again to get the Flux metamagic to conceal his astral comings and goings from the Black Lodge, who almost surely have a ritual sample of his. Dig also levels up his astral combat with Dog’s approval, to better fight these disutbing rift creatures. Checking the team file, they can find no mention of any of them from the Denver section, the last time sporadic rifts were spitting forth monsters and sucking civilians back through.

Ringmaster says he needs them to keep looking, which they plan to do after Dig does his monthly magical order meeting. First, however, they are summoned to a meeting with Brunwyn on her boat. They all attend besides CG, who is busy building up and using his new magical lodge at Plat Zone, and Cholula, who is still concerned about being captured and exploited by corps as a specimen. The rest of the team goes to the meet and gets a light warning about interfering too much in SK internal business- Brunwyn seems amused with how all the squabbling of the mortal employees in town has gone up to this point, but wants to make a point that she is paying attention and has noticed the team’s involvement.

She turns to walk away, dismissing them, and DIg having missed the point asks “so why did you ask us to meet?” after which Creep has to intercede and explain what just happened, after which they exit, with Baritone also telling them they’ll likely receive some more work soon. They head back to the Plat Zone, and rest up while Dig heads out for his meeting, before they head back out to get to the bottom of these rifts…..

Rescue Mission and Ritual Disruption
10/30/2023, 11:51:52 AM
Runners
Creep
Chew Gum
Dig
Otters aka Roadkill
Tabasco aka Cholula

The team, newly tipped off to some strange rituals occuring around town (performed by mysterious cloaked figures) head out to investigate, unable to find any initial traces of ChewGum. Cholula hangs back initially to finish healing, while TD keeps helping out in Hamtramck. Dig, Otters, and Creep navigate their way over the partially blown bridges to Windsor, which is starting to feel a bit separate- the physical difficulties of travel there currently manifesting in some slight cultural drift.

They head to the necropolis and check on CG’s bolthole there, finding all his stuff intact, but some evidence that someone had been there before them- a few well-concealed trackers, and some kind of hard to see trick with the door that they didn’t spot in time. Worried about who might come looking, they hid and surveil for a while, and eventually see a watcher spirit turn up, see them, and flee before they can engage. Dig tries to track it but something obscures his ability to do so. They are concerned that this watcher looks similar or perhaps identical to the one that was following Bloodripper when they ambushed him.

After waiting long enough (and getting enough drones on surveillance) to be convinced noone else is coming right now, they track down the ritual site – at an intersection of leylines in the cemetery complex. Dig checks it out, and Creep doses himself with some eye drops provided by the Walker in Ways, which allow anyone with their natural eyes to gain astral perception for 1 day/night rotation, with some costs.

The ritual looks like a large force endeavor, with a ton of reagents expended, and a sacrifice component, though someone has cleaned up the astral area since, making it hard to get specifics. Dig thinks he could recognize the cast if he encounters them or their signature. While checking the expended reagents for evidence, Dig recognizes some as of a brand he’s bought from Grandmother Biyu before, and heads over to her base to enquire.

Creep and Otters head back to Detroit proper on foot to meet up. Dig confers with Biyu, and she confirms that the reagents are hers, processed and sold at a Triad facility on the Dockfront. She gives Dig the location and calls ahead to tell them to cooperate. Dig flies over after radio-ing the others to meet him there. They link back up at the facility, and after some initial pushback the on-site Triad security let them talk to one of the clean-suited techs working there, who pulls up their purchase receipts, and confirms that they have sold a few large batches recently to the same person: Liam the Black.

They say he seemed kind of sleazy and they were surprised he had the kind of resources needed, but he had paid in full. The team heads back to the Plat Zone, and confers with Ringmaster, who tells them that Liam is a burnt-out street mage with no real pull except his natural sleazy charisma, who has apparently collected a small cult, but is not regarded by Ringmaster (or others) as a serious player in town. He is able to provide a location for the cult’s base though.

The team heads over and surveils it for a while, watching as the small group becomes increasingly debauched while Liam promises over and over that they’ll all be magical again soon (worrying). Eventually, everyone at the house crashes, and Otters sneaks a drone in, drugs Liam, and leaves him vulnerable to Dig’s mind probe, with which they get a lot of useful detail.

Apparently, Liam has been purchasing the reagents on the behalf of a woman named Helene Baptiste, who looks down on him, but is convinced he’s successfully using her to gain his power back as he believes she is connected to the Black Lodge. They get a few different locations from his mind where he’s made deliveries, including near the Windsor necropolis, so they think he must be delivering near the planned ritual sites, since Baptists seems not to think much of his acuity or consider him a risk or threat.

Not having anything else useful in his brain, they leave him and the cult be, but Otters and Creep stick around to watch and see if they lead them anywhere while Dig goes to check the other ritual sites. He finds more evidence of massive rituals, made to trap or contain something powerful, using tons of reagents plus sacrifice, and then astrally cleansed. Checking in with Ringmaster again, they hear of rumors of missing street mages around town, and get worried that CG might be the next likely victim for the upcoming ritual.

All they know is that it is at the University by Bloomfield, so they head up there and poke around, pretty quickly finding a leyline convergence at the library, and spotting a ritual in progress in the basement, which is closed to the public currently. Not wanting to step on any toes, Otters calls up Ronnie’s Raiders to warn them that the team is gonna have to do an op on their turf, while Creep sneaks in through the vents with mouse Dig and some of Otters’ drones.

Creep is able to get into the ritual chamber and slip mouse Dig out of a vent, while spotting 6 mages chanting in Latin and walking along a large ritual circle around a nude CG who is bound to a stone slab, seemingly magically summoned up from the poured concrete floor of the chamber. He seems drugged but alive, so Dig dashes over to him, slipping inside the magic circle without getting noticed, and uses his mindlink spell to get into communication with CG.

Creep relays all this to Otters, who is able to use it as proof of nefarious happenings in his meeting with Ronnie and Prof. Vinh Trong, who both seem confused and upset at the news. Ronnie calls an emergency meeting of the Raiders to try to coordinate an evacuation of civilians from the immediate area, while Trong starts pondering containment spells in case something goes wrong, and requests that they attempt to preserve the library.

Cholula gets eyes on some guards in armored suits watching the basement stairs, and sees them get a call just after this, with one of them heading downstairs to warn the ritualists. Conferring mentally, Dig and CG determine that if they can take out the ritual leader the force of it should harm the other mages present, and will at least be a good distraction. Using a consensual mind probe, Dig is able to identify which cloaked figure is Baptiste, the leader, from CG’s drugged memories. As Otters rushes back and gets the rest of her drones in position, they launch their attack.

Creep shoots Baptists, blasting through her armored cloak and magical protections, just before Otters darts her with two snake drones, flat-lining her. This causes the desired magical feedback as the remaining mages struggle to hold control of the ritual. Creep shoots another one of them, further hindering their ability to do so, and one of them lets out a frantic “oh no!”. Meanwhile, Cholula and one of the Mules have gotten into a fight with guards up top but manage to drop them quickly, and get the basement stair door open using one of their keycards.

Dig pulls CG free of the slab and his restraints using his magic hand, and starts hauling both of them towards the stairs when the ritual goes fully out of control and they are both pulled into astral space. They see a portal to some deep, distant point, but are more distracted by seeing Dig’s mentor, Dog, being pulled apart into multiple different aspects or segments and seemingly scattered. Dig hears a distressed whimper as this occurs. CG decides to stay astral for now, as he seems more functional there, but Dig is able to focus and maintain his spell, and keep hauling them to the stairs, which allows him to return primarily to his body.

The team all exfils up the stairs and out the front as they feel magical pressure building behind them, and then with a large pop, a huge section of the library building explodes outwards (seemingly only contained by Trong and some assistant’s frantic efforts) before imploding back in on itself, leaving a massive scooped out crater. The team sticks around to “help” but doesn’t have much to do. They try to warn Trong and others of the Black Lodge threat, but he seems to honestly believe it is just a conspiracy theory.

Ronnie seems a little more receptive, but hints that the team should be on their way since they just ran out of the library right before it exploded. CG finally rallies from his stupor as the drug wears off, and he and Dig are a little surprised when Freddy Bales seems to recognize mouse Dig and comes over to talk to them. Cholula, suspicious, tries to plant a tracker on Bales after he makes some pleasantries and then leaves to try to help Trong with magical cleanup, but Bales not only notices the tracker but almost manages to sneak it back onto Cholula. Detente.

The team heads back to the Plat Zone, and shares their intel with Ringmaster, who seems very worried and says he need to look into some things. Dig rushes them out the door again, hearing another astral whimper, to see what happened to Dog and if they can do anything. CG also wants to swing by his bolthole to grab his stuff. They head back to Windsor, and CG gets re-equipped, finding a few more tracking tags in the process as they do a more thorough search.

They also find a rippling magical dome at the previously examined ritual site. Dig manipulates his aura to appear like Helene Baptiste’s, and after a few attempts, gain entry. He find an aspect of Dog (pack mentality) here, and manages to call on his connection to his mentor to get this aspect to trust him and follow him out of the circle, after which it quickly vanishes, though distressingly it seems to have been made to manifest as the other team members can all briefly see it also. Being further concerned, Dig urges speed to the next few ritual sites.

At the next, on the waterfront, they find another magic dome, this one having attracted a bit of a crowd. CG takes some quicksilver pics, while Dig once again enters as Baptiste and manages to lure this aspect of Dog (play and chasing) out, almost getting bitten in his mouse form to do so. When the plates develop, CG spots that same watcher spirit observing them again, but it is gone by the time they look in astral…. this is starting to get suspicious. They head to the next site, some woods near the Blight, and find another trapped aspect (loyalty and friendship), which Dig again calls on his ties to his mentor to entice out.

Feeling like they’ve discovered about 3/4 of the fractured Dog, they head back to the Plat Zone to inquire about fresh leads, and hear of one more rumored ritual site in the DDB. They leave Ringmaster their data and the quicksilver plates so he can make some calls, and head down. They eventually track their way to the site where they fought the escaped lab experiment dog of Aurand’s, and find another magic dome there.

This is Dog’s protectiveness/aggression, and it is very backed in a corner with hackles up, giving off intensely scary growls. Otters says she’s got this, and Dig pulls her through the barrier, again posing as Baptiste. Otters totally confidently approaches, grabs Dog by the scruff, and firmly walks it out, with Dig pulling them through again. With this last aspect of Dog free and the 4 pieces reunited, Dig feels his connections to his mentor return, and both he and Otters feels that they can call upon Dog for direct assistance in return, as Dog seems unusually locally present still.

Heading back to the Plat Zone, they once again debrief Ringmaster and he shares some intel he got from the outside, riskily using TM’s to do so, bypassing the Null data blockade via Resonance dives. Based on the ritual used, and how it all worked, and the fact that it was able to make a mentor spirit manifest and the break it up to trap it, they collectively speculate that the Black Lodge may have been trying to splinter and capture a Horror, which might have been what they were preparing to summon. Or, this may have all just been a test run.

Once they start speculating, though, they realize that the Black Lodge may have been behind much of what’s been going on in Detroit, as a blacked out post-corporate city would be the perfect place to run a bunch of dark magic experiments. The team wonders if the BL perhaps was assisting the Malakim in harming ARES internally, ensuring both their weakening and their disastrous move, as well as if the BL was quietly boosting the Shadow, Famine, and other dark magic cults to try to create or summon a powerful spirit (maybe even a spirit of the city) that they could bind.

With this intel, plus some other Blackout knowledge, as well as some other tidbits and overall synthesis of what’s going on, the team decides to pull the trigger on the CEO Tarot card. Otters presses the button, and a logo appears in her RCC of each of the Big 10 Mega’s, with a small inset of their current CEO. After some debate, they decide to go with AZT, as they and Ringmaster agree that either AZT or SK are the two least likeliest to be infiltrated by the BL at their highest levels, SK cause of Dragons, AZT cause of 4th World enmities between factions of immortal humans.

Otters presses the button, and Flavia de la Rosa picks up. She seems intrigued but not patient, and Otters gives a quick synopsis of how she got her number, and what she and the team can offer. de la Rosa is interested enough to stay on the line, and seems like a skilled social operator, using effective mirroring and asking insightful questions as Otters shared the agreed upon paydata.

Asking what they want in return, de la Rosa is happy to offer eventual transport out, saying that there is a lot going on in Denver that needs looking into, which seems like a perfect place for a skilled team to travel next. She offers them a vacation diversion on the way, but Otters, having heard tell of the team’s last vacation disaster, demurs. They also get AZT to agree to air drop in food, to be distro’ed by G’ma Biyu but with AZT logos, which will be good PR. de la Rosa is also happy to promise some future equipment to get them geared up for their Denver trip, once the city reopens.

She also sends along a news update packet at Otters’ request, to get caught up on the outside world. Saying they likely will not speak again, she instructs them to turn to Cehualli for other local needs in the meantime and cuts the call. Interestingly, only the AZT icon vanishes from Otters’ RCC, so it seems like the other 9 CEO’s are still on the table for future calls, though punching this one through the Resonance was all that Ringmaster’s comms team can manage for now.

With that having gone fairly well, the team debates what to do with all the intense news they received in the packet- Seattle is officially free! The UCAS seems to be in a state of collapse! The Returned and Chimeras are popping up everywhere! Dig wants to broadcast it all on a public wideband, but they hold off for now to debate a bit first.

The team wakes up in the safehouse, with DIg having some disturbing memories of hyper-realistic dreams of vine-strewn deep matrix locales. The smell of frying flesh suffuses the van. The other paralyzed captive watches in horror.

Viking Cowgirl is the leader of the Blackout Boyz, or at least the one in charge of coordinating today’s actions. Both factions have been heavily depleted by the team’s recent attacks, and have worked together before but are both paid by some unknown outside party. The goal of today’s attack was to destroy district infrastructure, a project the toxies are often paid for (they’ve been behind the mesh network attacks), and also to capture specific targets if spotted- these being all 6 members of the team.

They had also been paid, alongside other local mages of questionable morality, to prevent or destroy any astral forms attempting to get messages into or out of Detroit, ensuring a near total information blockade, with the matrix down and MET2000 patrolling the land and sea exits. They’d also worked some additional jobs as backup for the Blackout Boyz but don’t know further details. It seems like the only survivors of this group are Mother Gorgon and the cultists with her.

This, suddenly, makes the team worry about where CG is, as he hasn’t turned up yet. They race over to the other side of the district (not very far), and find signs of a battle but no ongoing combat. Dig recovers the burnt hilt of CG’s flaming sword but it seems inert, so Otters gives it to a local kid who they find and question who saw some stuff.

But, distracted by that angry spirit trapped within the blade, he was unable to also defend himself against the attackers and was KO’ed and captured- they called off their assault and fled while the rest of the team was still bogged down in combat on the other side of the area.

Not having any better leads at the moment, they check on the neighborhood, with TD saying they will focus on this for now. Finding no further attackers or threats, they tell everyone to come out and start rebuilding, and find that luckily casualties were pretty low- mostly Children of Loxley who were on patrol and whatever random civilians had the bad luck to be out in the open, as well as the occasional stray bullet or spell that punched through a wall or window.

Dig, slightly rested, and with the benefit of the secondary effect of the Gamma Scopolamine, is ready to question the second subject. Otters tries the social route first, without much success, but gets a little info. the BB’s seem to view themselves as guns for hire just trying to survive, who scored a sweet contract which also ensures their families/dependents are provided for (if local). He seems to think that the team is going to torture and kill him, based on what he has seen so far, and Otters doesn’t really convince him otherwise, so he isn’t overly cooperative.

Feeling they’ve hit a dead end, Dig gets the others to hold him and lands on his head, doing another Mind Probe. Things the team learns: they don’t know who they’re working for, or where they deliver the TM’s. due to liberal use of Laës provided by Stanli the Candyman, who also takes it to forget the transaction. They also don’t know where their families actually are, though they get regular trid calls (the team worries these could be faked easily by Null Sect). They were behind the assassination of various scientists that Brunwyn was trying to acquire.

The UCAS has declared martial law, due to the Blackouts, and has cut off aid to ALL former ARES locales- including Detroit, declaring it ARES’ responsibility to care for their citizens and former employees. ARES has also stopped supplying any aid to any UCAS locales, claiming it is the government’s responsibility. This leaves a lot of newly ex-corpos in a real shit situation- arcologies and facilities with no power, food, or water coming in etc. Also, there are some disturbing rumors of some III Corps members returning, often very confused and distant…

They call into Ringmaster, and ask to come to the Plat Zone to debrief, and head over. Once there, they decide they better actually fully relocate for now, just in case, so Otters spends some time hauling drones and gear over to a Plat Zone apartment while Creep and Cholula get healed up in the basement clinic and Dig rests from all his spellcasting. Once they resettle, they debrief with Ringmaster, and he shares some info as well: he believes that the Grid Pirates/Blackout Boyz are attempting a theoretical ritual to alter the “Sprite of the Grid”, effectively a matrix equivalent to a “Spirit of the City”.

He explains that this is a fairly new concept, revolving around the “noosphere” as an equivalent to astral space, and based on the severely limited comms he’s been able to get in/out via brave TM’s risking Null Sect attack- it seems that even with the matrix down, Resonance wells can still be found. He also says he’s heard some other reports of the III Corps vets and others, which are becoming collectively known as “the Returned”. He shares some rumors of anachronistic figures appearing who seem to have disappeared in both recent and much older history (Yucatan rebels and WWII soldiers from the Pacific theater), as well as some III Corps vets returning with what sound like chimeric mods like what the team encountered outside Site 000 in PA.

This is all very upsetting, and Ringmaster offers to pay the team to stay involved, though they settle for healing + safehouse for this batch of info. Dig flies up to G’ma Biyu and debriefs her on the latest re: vans, he also tells her where to pick up Father Gorgon’s body, which she says she should be able to process into some interesting reagents. She pays them another installment for their ongoing investigation, and also says she’s heard some rumors of a strange cloaked group doing small, dark rituals in obscure places around town. Dig returns to the team with this news, just as they get a radio call from Jaga at the water treatment plant.

She says that during their ritual a few nights back, they spotted a strange mana spike from the direction of the Windsor cemetery (where CG has a safehouse)- it looked like some bad mojo but came and went pretty quickly, and they’ve been too occupied to investigate it further. The team decides to look into it, though Cholula stays behind to heal more, still being pretty wrecked. Otters is also feeling antagonistic towards CG, in a way that seems unusual for her, and the team speculates that the love potion may have worn off. What this means for CG, who drank one of the love potions as well, they do not know! But they hope that perhaps investigating these dark rituals will lead them closer to finding him, as they have no current leads…

Cholula helps her descend from the rooftop, and they get picked up by some Plat Zone runners in one of the jury-rigged chem fuel vans.

Back at the Plat Zone, some of them rest while the rest review Cholula’s footage. They get face images of most of the runners they saw, and pass those along to Ringmaster to try to ID, and TD suggests also comparing to the “missing persons” message boards at various enclaves. They also spot some interesting details: there are no TM’s, or space to detain TM’s or other prisoners anywhere onsite. There are old mechanical hookups in one corner of the garage, though they didn’t get a great look at them. There is a banner with “Blackout Boyz” spray-painted on it. There are 12 maps up on the walls with routes and timetables, and a roving schedule of which team drives which route- implying they’ve taken out about half of their operating strength.

And perhaps most interestingly, there are plentiful supplies down here, all bearing the insignia of the Freedom Network, who the team knows from Seattle is often used as an unwitting meatspace cutout by the Null, whether directly or via their street wing, Popular Will. As they’d previously been involved with TM kidnapping and Null brain-in-jar experiments and such in Seattle at least, this all checks out, and gives more suggestions as to how this whole op works. However, this looks like it was just a staging ground for the vans, so there must be at least one more facility….

Also the lack of comms gear and astral barrier (earth and glowmoss panels) that concealed leave Otters wondering how they receive updated instructions, and she starts wondering about the route the vans are driving, and how the Null is moving about with no active matrix, other than the hyperlocal signals from the vans. Knowing that the van route traces old 1.0 Matrix infrastructure routes, she starts to worry that the Null are using those to travel and communicate, and decides to make another visit to the Run and Sollars library soon to look for old maps and layout and stuff, so they can start cutting off those pathways.

They get a radio call from Octave, reporting that Creep barely survived, but she had to do some radical cyber-mods to put him back together, so all of his limbs are detachable now. He’s not yet conscious, and Cholula and Dig are also still both banged up, so they spend the day charging tech, patching armor and resting. Creep finally flies home the next day, with some new cyber and a lower essence, and they all start catching up on how wild that mission was.

Before they can get to deep, though, CG races inside to sound of gunfire and screams from nearby, and frantically announces: “Hamtramck is under attack!!”

The team finally follows up on CG’s famine investigation, and calls up Pretty out at the relocated Long Pig Farms.
